## Prioritization Lens & Strategic Frame (added 2026-07-28 — read before scoping or reprioritizing anything below)

> ### Ratified product vision — 2026-08-05. Read this first; it governs everything below.
>
> **NoteLib is a learning system built on top of a knowledge library.** The library is the foundation; **the learning journey is the product.**
>
> Three layers, earned strictly in order:
>
> 1. **Trust** — comprehensive Official Review Sets, so a learner can say *"I can trust NoteLib to cover my review."*
> 2. **Habit** — Study Packs, Companion, Progress, and Review Sets forming the system they return to.
> 3. **Community** — high-quality user-created and shared knowledge, once the first two are earned.
>
> **This confirms and sharpens the "journey, not the note" frame below rather than replacing it.** What it adds is the *ordering*, and one prioritization consequence: **community content is no longer the primary acquisition strategy.** Long-term, users creating and sharing their own notes is still absolutely the goal — the vision is not abandoned, it is being earned in the correct order. Only after a learner trusts NoteLib's coverage does the product earn the right to become their personal knowledge library.
>
> **What this means when scoping:** work that deepens Official Review Set comprehensiveness or the learning system outranks work that grows user-generated content as a funnel. Do not propose UGC as top-of-funnel acquisition; do not read the de-emphasis as the community feature being cut. Full statement in `docs/product/SPEC.md` → Product Philosophy.

This section exists so a fresh session doesn't have to re-derive from scratch what "NoteLib's center of gravity has shifted toward the learner's journey, not the note" actually means for prioritization. It was added after a strategic reprioritization discussion prompted by noticing that recent releases (Dashboard, Progress, Companion, Coach, Official Review Sets, Reusable Practice Assets) skew journey-centric rather than notes-first.

**This is not a new pivot — it's a long-held bet becoming visible, and internal strategy is already ahead of it.** "A Note Collection is a trackable readiness journey, not a folder" has been the stated direction since v0.33.0; "NoteLib as a personal learning OS" was ratified internally with GPT on 2026-07-23 (5 days before this note), as part of the Company Redefinition effort (see that section above). What changed isn't the direction — it's that enough journey-layer surface area now exists to see the shape in aggregate. Treat this as confirmation the existing direction is compounding, not a trigger to re-litigate it.

**The one real, un-actioned gap this surfaced: external marketing copy hasn't caught up to internal strategy.** The landing page still says "your notes become your study system" (notes-first); internal strategy has said learning-OS-centric for 5 days. See the Backlog Index row below — since superseded by the **Messaging Architecture** row. **Two parts of this note are now out of date:** the conversion-test condition was explicitly lifted for positioning copy by owner ratification 2026-08-01 (narrative consistency with the product vision is a design decision, not an optimization experiment; conversion is measured post-launch instead of gating rollout — pricing/checkout *mechanics* changes still need evidence), and "small, not urgent" undersold it — the ratified initiative is product-wide. The landing page itself is still un-actioned and is now tracked in that row's remaining-surfaces list.

**Prioritization filter — use to triage new candidates, weighted, not flat.** Does a candidate move: (1) acquisition, (2) return rate, (3) study effectiveness, (4) reusable practice assets, (5) the creator ecosystem, or (6) does it fix something broken / protect trust (bug fixes, data integrity, paywall correctness — always in scope regardless of the other five)? While retention (2.4% W1→W2, unmoved across three prior attempts) remains the binding constraint, (2) should dominate; (1) and (3)-(5) are tie-breakers, not co-equal boxes to check. Don't let "this touches box N a little" alone justify a build — that's how feature creep re-enters.

**Guardrail: don't let Challenge Quiz's recent momentum keep pulling the next discretionary pick.** The heavy Challenge Quiz investment this cycle (v0.60.1/v0.60.2/v0.60.3/v0.61.0) was mostly bug-driven, not strategy-driven — v0.60.0's template sharing surfaced a real production bug cluster that had to get fixed, and familiarity kept pulling more work into that same corner afterward. That work was necessary and is now done; Challenge Quiz is stable. The next discretionary pick after v0.61.0 should default toward between-session/Coach/Progress work (the actual proven bottleneck — see the "Product Learning Loop" section at the bottom of this doc) unless a new production bug forces otherwise.

**Loops, not just features — see the updated "Product Learning Loop" section at the bottom of this doc**, which now names three loops (Acquisition, Learning, Creator) and is honest about which ones are actually running today versus asserted or unproven. Use it as a diagnostic — naming something a loop doesn't make it spin.

## Backlog Index (read this before deciding what's next — do not scan prose to find candidates)

**Invariant: no PLANNING OR CURATION ARTIFACT may exist anywhere in `docs/` without a row here** — `docs/claude-prompt/*-out/` directories, `docs/claude-plans/` files, `docs/claude-findings/` reports, and `docs/curriculum/` workbooks and queries alike. **⚠️ THE RULE IS STATED BY PROPERTY NOW, BECAUSE NAMING DIRECTORIES HAS BEEN OUTGROWN TWICE.** It first named only `*-out/`, and the `v0.71.1` kickoff found five unindexed `docs/claude-plans/` files; it was widened to name that directory too, and the `v0.97.0` signoff then found six unindexed files in a **new** `docs/curriculum/`, which the rule did not mention. A list of directories is always one new directory behind. **The test is what the file IS — does anything remain to be done with it — not where it lives.** A Fable session that writes planning output adds its row in the same commit. This rule exists because Smart Review Planning (7 fully-architected documents in `docs/claude-prompt/fable-out/`) sat undiscovered for ~5 release cycles, found only by chance when the user asked about it directly — a prose mention buried in a released-version paragraph is not an index entry.

**One narrow exemption, stated 2026-08-10 at the `v0.71.1` kickoff rather than left to be re-derived: a *release artifact* is covered by its version's own row and does not need one of its own.** A release artifact is a file written to serve one release's decision and then finished — a consultation prompt sent to GPT, a sizing query run once against production, an audit result. It is not planning output waiting to be scheduled, which is what this invariant exists to catch. The distinction is *does anything remain to be done with it*, not which directory it lives in. Currently exempt, each traceable through its release's section above: `b2-migration-artifact-sizing.sql` and `c1-multi-program-null-domain-sizing.sql` (`v0.71.0`), **⚠️ plus two that were exempt and are now DELETED in the `v0.97.0` docs cleanup, 2026-08-29** — `b2-learner-owned-applicable-programs-consultation-prompt.md` and `onboarding-intent-router-product-ux-consultation-prompt.md`, both `v0.71.0`, both answered and shipped (B2 became `ADR-001` → *Curation authority*; the Intent Router became `v0.71.0` slice 5, whose live row cites its **plan** file and audit SQL, not the prompt). **⚠️ THIS SENTENCE IS A LIVE REGISTRY, NOT A DATED LOG, which is why it is edited rather than left standing** — the historical-log-entry ruling (leave dated scan notes intact) does not extend to a list that asserts what is exempt *now*; leaving it would have claimed exemption for files that no longer exist. Also `item1-admin-authored-applicable-programs-sizing.sql` (`v0.71.1`, run 2026-08-11, results recorded in `ADR-001` → *Curation authority*), plus `v0.67.0-explore-owns-discovery-ia.md` (`v0.67.0`, shipped in full). **If a `.sql` or consultation prompt ever carries an unrun query or an unanswered question past its release's signoff, it stops being an artifact and needs a row.**

**Cite rows by TITLE, never by line number.** Added 2026-08-14 after the `v0.75.0` pressure test found three citations pointing at the wrong rows: `RELEASES.md` and this file cited "row 185" and "row 187", both correct when written, both wrong once the kickoff commit inserted two lines above the index. **This recurs by construction** — every kickoff edits text above this table — and one of the broken pointers was the stated evidence for a design decision, so the reader would have landed on an unrelated row and found no support for it.

**Review ritual:** every `/kickoff`, scan this table — bump `Last reviewed`, check whether any `Gate` condition became true, **apply the consolidation rule below to any checkpoint written since the last kickoff** (a new dated read joins an existing batch date unless it earns its own by the stated criteria), verify every `docs/claude-prompt/` planning directory **and every `docs/claude-plans/` file** still has a row or falls under the artifact exemption above (see kickoff checklist step 8 in `CLAUDE.md`), and scan for `CHECKPOINT` rows past their due date (see kickoff checklist step 9).

**Signoff scan, `v0.99.0`, 2026-08-31.** Step 8: **two new `docs/claude-plans/` artifacts arrived
after the kickoff scan** — `review-sets-first-class-and-ai-language-audit.md` and
`review-sets-audit-gpt-tightening-prompt.md`, both indexed by the session that produced them, with an
addendum left beneath the kickoff note rather than rewriting it. **⚠️ THEY WERE SPOTTED AS UNTRACKED
FILES DURING THIS SIGNOFF, WHICH IS THE LATEST POSSIBLE MOMENT** — step 8 runs at the NEXT kickoff, so
an artifact landing mid-release is only caught by someone noticing. `docs/curriculum/` is outside the
scan entirely by decision (durable tools, not plans with open loops), recorded in its README.
Step 9: **nothing overdue.** The only pre-today date is the struck-through `[CHECKPOINT — due
2026-08-18]`, resolved 2026-08-04. **⚠️ ONE CHECKPOINT GAINED A CAVEAT RATHER THAN A DATE:**

The `v0.82.0-post-deploy-query-*.csv` family remains the standing exempt false positive — **eleventh kickoff running where the CHECK failed and the invariant held.** Step 9: **nothing overdue.** The only pre-today dates are struck through — `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-08-18]` (resolved 2026-08-04, re-verified for the tenth kickoff running) and `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-01]` (closed *VERDICT PASS*, read early 2026-08-19).

**⚠️ THE CLUSTER FIGURE CARRIED BY THE LAST FOUR SCAN NOTES WAS WRONG, AND IT WAS WRONG BY CONSTRUCTION.** Every scan from `v0.103.0` through `v0.106.0` recorded *"27 rows on `2026-09-11`"*. **That is a MENTION count, not a row count** — a `grep -c` over a file whose Backlog Index intro is itself thick with prose cross-references to those dates, so the scan notes were counting their own predecessors. Re-derived here by parsing the table body row by row (159 table lines; **142 live, 9 struck**) and reading each row's own title and `Gate` cell: **32 LIVE rows touch `2026-09-10`–`2026-09-17`**, and the useful split is not the total but the KIND — **~12 are CHECKPOINT READS** (production SQL with kill criteria already written: `v0.72.0` retention H1+H5 on `2026-09-10`; `v0.74.0` ×2 on `2026-09-12`; `v0.79.0`, `v0.78.0`, `v0.80.0` on `2026-09-14`; `v0.81.0` on `2026-09-15`; `v0.82.0` ×2, `v0.83.0`, `v0.84.0` on `2026-09-16`; `v0.85.0` on `2026-09-17`) and **~20 are PARKED WORK ITEMS** the window unblocks (the whole onboarding set, Target Audience Phases 2–4, Discovery System, the LaTeX rendering gap). **⚠️ THE DISTINCTION IS THE POINT: the cluster is a READING workload, not a building one**, which is what made it safe to scope a feature release across it rather than reserving a slot. **Recorded at length because a wrong number was carried forward four times unchallenged, and it was the stated input to a sequencing decision each time** — the exact status-staleness failure this intro keeps warning about, appearing inside step 9's own bookkeeping for the second time.

**⚠️ ONE LOAD-BEARING FINDING, UN-ACTIONED SINCE IT WAS WRITTEN, AND IT IS FOLDED INTO THIS RELEASE.** The ratified *Adaptive Practice becomes the recommendation engine* row records that **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-12]`'s kill criterion instructs a reader to "restore a demoted entry point"** if Adaptive Practice adoption fell — which **directly contradicts the direction that row ratifies**, and which `v0.107.0` item 2 implements. The row's own words: it *"must be re-specified now, or it will fire in September and someone will correctly follow an instruction that undoes a ratified direction."* **Nobody re-specified it, and it fires in 9 days, inside this release's window.** It is folded in as item 3 — **docs-only, and as a DEPENDENCY of item 2 rather than a third feature.** **⚠️ Note what this means about the gate: step 9 scans for OVERDUE rows, so a checkpoint that is merely WRONG scans clean until the day it fires.** It was found by reading the direction row against its checkpoint, not by the date scan.

The row is amended to carry that.

**Gate types, added 2026-07-28 (pressure-tested via advisor + an independent Opus review — see the "Prioritization Lens" section above for why this was needed).** Every `Gate` cell below should read as one of four types, tagged inline. The problem this fixes: an EVIDENCE gate and a DECISION gate both render as "blocked," but only one is actually waiting on data — a DECISION gate is unblockable by more querying, it just needs the owner to choose. Conflating them makes the whole roadmap feel uniformly data-blocked when a real chunk of it is one decision away.

- **`[EVIDENCE]`** — genuinely needs production data or a real-world signal before building. The strict-gating discipline applies in full.
- **`[DECISION]`** — waiting on an explicit owner call, not on more data. Actionable today by having the conversation, not by running a query.
- **`[EFFORT]`** — needs a scoping pass, curator capacity, or non-engineering execution (outreach, a design pass). Not data-blocked, resource- or attention-blocked.
- **`[CHECKPOINT — due YYYY-MM-DD]`** — an item that shipped ahead of evidence (an `EVIDENCE` gate was invalid — see the bootstrap test below) and carries a dated, falsifiable post-build obligation instead of a pre-build gate. Must have all five: a date, a named kill criterion, live in this column (not buried in Status prose), scanned by the review ritual above, and its measurement instrumentation shipped in the same release — a checkpoint without a working metric is decorative (see `08-diagnostic-read-methodology.md`'s v0.48.0 digest-lift postmortem for what an unmeasurable checkpoint looks like in practice).

**The bootstrap test — when an `EVIDENCE` gate is actually invalid and the item should ship as `DECISION` + `CHECKPOINT` instead.** All three must hold, not just one:
1. The feature itself is the mechanism that would produce the gate's own metric (not merely correlated with it) — e.g. Knowledge Impact is the only lever that rewards publishing, so waiting for publish-rate evidence before building the thing that would move publish rate is circular. (Contrast: Bulk Quiz's teacher-count gate is *not* invalid this way — teachers sign up for reasons unrelated to Bulk Quiz, so "we can't know until it exists" doesn't apply.)
2. It fits one release slot and is reversible — no schema/pricing/nav commitment that needs a migration to withdraw. This clause is what stops the bootstrap argument from justifying unlimited spend.
3. The checkpoint metric will actually be operable at current scale in the stated window — not still a single-digit denominator when the date arrives. This is the clause that would have caught Knowledge Impact's original re-check design (re-running the same 3-creator-count query) before it shipped; the fix is a conditional-rate event (did a creator who viewed their impact dashboard publish again within N days), not a raw count.

**⚠️ CHECKPOINT CONSOLIDATION — 2026-08-16. Read this before acting on any dated row below.** The table had accumulated **~24 dated obligations across 18 rows, spread over nine separate September dates.** That is not a discipline, it is overhead: nine reading sessions against one analytics table, in one window, at a scale where most of the rates involved have denominators in the tens. **This applies clause 3 of the bootstrap test retroactively** — it was always the rule (*"operable at current scale … not still a single-digit denominator when the date arrives"*), it was simply never enforced against checkpoints already written.

**Nine dates collapse to two. The per-row metrics, kill criteria and caveats below all still stand — only the date changes.**

- **`2026-09-11` — the onboarding funnel read, alone.** It keeps its own date because it has a real denominator (375 signups against a 62.4% completion baseline), it gates concrete work (the onboarding catalog-first follow-up), and reading it early is worthless. Rows: onboarding redesign, catalog-first-in-onboarding.
- **`2026-09-16` — everything else, as ONE batch.** Queries are already written in `docs/claude-plans/september-2026-checkpoint-reads.sql`. **Query 0 (delivery health) is read first and conditions all the rest**, which is itself an argument for batching: run separately, eight of these reads are interpreted before the number that tells you whether their events even arrived. Rows: `v0.78.0`, `v0.79.0` (proximal), `v0.80.0`, `v0.81.0`, `v0.82.0`, `v0.74.0` ×3, post-mastery next step, Adaptive Practice entry attribution.
- **Unchanged:** distal reads (`2026-10-15`, `2026-11-09`), far-future architectural dates (`2027-02-01`), and the `2026-09-30` Challenge Quiz adoption reads — different windows, not the same batch.

**What earns its own date from here on.** Being wrong is expensive and hard to reverse (a migration that writes data, anything that destroys evidence — `v0.82.0`'s is the model), **or** the denominator is genuinely large (funnel rates over all signups, total event volume). **What does not:** a conversion rate on a surface only a handful of learners reach. Write those down, batch them, and accept that *"too small to read"* is the likely finding — it is still a finding, it just does not deserve a calendar entry of its own.

**⚠️ Two gates were converted to recorded confounds rather than kept, 2026-08-16 (owner call).** The Target Audience retirement and the academic-levels program cleanup were both gated on `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-13]` because they change Public Library discovery. **They now ship without waiting**, with the confound written down instead — the same call `v0.78.0` made and recorded. The reasoning: since `v0.79.0` the course/program chips are the primary filter vocabulary and audience is secondary, so removing it is a marginal confound rather than a fatal one. **This does NOT extend to dropping `notes.target_profile_type`**, which stays until `2026-09-16` reports — it is the input `V117` derived depth from, and dropping it would make that checkpoint's own kill criterion unexecutable.

Nobody knows how often that fires. | **⚠️ INSTRUMENTATION SHIPPED IN THE SAME COMMIT AND IS VERIFIED EMITTING, not merely enum-resident.** `BOARD_EXAM_STARTED` on the sampled path now carries `sourceScope`, `sourceCount`, `questionCount` and `expectedQuestionCount`; `startSession_boardExamStartEventCarriesTheMetricItsCheckpointReads` pins all four and was mutation-verified (forcing `sourceScope` to `manual` fails it). **⚠️ WITHOUT `sourceScope` AND `expectedQuestionCount` THIS ROW WOULD HAVE BEEN DECORATIVE** — `questionCount` alone cannot separate a sampled exam that assembled 12 from a legacy single-note one that only ever asked for 12, which is exactly the confusion the read must avoid. **READ:** among `BOARD_EXAM_STARTED` events with `sourceScope = plan`, the share where `questionCount < expectedQuestionCount`, plus the count of Board Exam sessions reaching `FAILED` with a reversal. **KILL CRITERION, stated before the read:** if **more than 30%** of plan-scoped starts ship short, the per-source fan-out cannot sustain a 30-item target and the response is to **lower `boardExamTargetQuestionCount` or harden per-source generation — NOT to weaken the floors**, which exist to stop a 4-question exam being sold as a board review. Separately, if **more than 10%** of plan-scoped starts FAIL outright, the floors are mis-set rather than the target. **⚠️ A CLEAN RESULT DOES NOT AUTHORISE RAISING THE TARGET** — exams assembling reliably at 30 makes the 50 / 75 / 100 question *live*, as its own decision with its own evidence; it is not a licence to raise it inside a later release's scope. **⚠️ DENOMINATOR CLAUSE:** Board Exam is PRO-only and capped at 10 sessions per month, so the population is small by construction. **Fewer than 20 plan-scoped starts in the window is a RE-DATE, not a verdict** — and note the second tier is deliberately absent: there is no larger funnel above Board Exam starts to read instead, so a proximal metric would measure something this row is not asking about. **⚠️ NO DEPLOY SPLIT APPLIES TO ANY EXISTING ROW:** this metric did not exist before this release, so there is no prior series for the new fields to interrupt. **~~⚠️ `[EFFORT]` — RE-ARM ONCE ANNOUNCED~~ ✅ RE-ARMED 2026-09-03, dated `2026-10-13`.** **Everything needed to re-arm is preserved above and must NOT be re-derived:** the read on `BOARD_EXAM_STARTED`'s `sourceScope` / `sourceCount` / `questionCount` / `expectedQuestionCount`, the kill criterion, the denominator clause, and the note that no deploy split applies. **⚠️ Those four field names and that event's firing condition remain frozen while this is lifted** — a lifted checkpoint still owns its metric, and changing the fields now would mean re-arming against a broken series. **⚠️ Do NOT read this metric in the meantime and treat a short-assembly count as evidence about the target.** | 2026-09-03 |

gate. | **⚠️ ARCHITECTURE AND SEQUENCE APPROVED 2026-09-02; ALL FOUR OWNER DECISIONS ANSWERED.** **(1) The Plus cap of 4 is REJECTED as arithmetic leakage** (`12/3`), not product design — preserve a The ratified reconciliation: *choose the **coarsest** existing Domain Context under which the material's appropriate treatment remains unchanged; a more specific context is justified only when that specificity materially changes the correct terminology, framing, examples, conventions or scope of the generated material.* Therefore: **multi-program applicability alone does NOT imply `ENGINEERING_SCIENCES`**; **membership of a Civil Engineering Review Set does NOT imply `CIVIL_ENGINEERING`**; **the existence of a narrower or program-shaped value does not mean it should be selected**; **Applicable Programs remains responsible for curriculum and discovery applicability**; and **specialization must be justified by a real difference in authoring treatment.** Water Supply Engineering and Water Treatment both classify as **`ENGINEERING_SCIENCES`** — no demonstrated treatment difference. **§B — ~~"leave it NULL" is ENFORCED IMPOSSIBLE for multi-program notes~~ — SUPERSEDED BY `v0.100.0` (owner ruling, 2026-09-01): the save-time block MOVED to generation time, so a multi-program note with a NULL Domain Context is now SAVEABLE and simply not generation-ready. directory's row, and **thinning a directory's contents while keeping its row is a different and riskier operation** that needs its own pass — if it is wanted at all. **Un-parks on an explicit decision to run that pass; nothing else re-opens this row.** **⚠️ The original decision text is retained below, because the reasoning is what future passes need:**  **(1) `claude-findings/` is EVIDENCE, not | **~~The recovery sweep never writes `generation_status_at`~~ — ALREADY FIXED, row was stale.** Closed 2026-08-26 | Found 2026-08-19 by read (d) of `docs/claude-plans/v0.86.0-checkpoint-read.sql`, then confirmed in code | **RESOLVED — and it was resolved before this row was last reviewed, which is the point worth recording.** `v0.89.0` shipped the fix (`CLAUDE.md` records it in that release's scope); `GenerationRecoveryRowWriter.recoverPool` now stamps `setGenerationStatusAt(OffsetDateTime.now(ZoneOffset.UTC))` beside the `FAILED` write. They are config-owned, so tightening is a config change, not a code change. | **Read 14 days after DEPLOY, not merge.** **KILL CRITERION, stated before the read:** if part (a) returns a non-zero count on any surface — any row still non-terminal past its own bound — the sweeper is **not working**, and the response is to diagnose it, **not** to tighten the bounds. **⚠️ DENOMINATOR CLAUSE:** if the backlog drained on the first post-deploy sweep and no deploy has interrupted generation since, then zero is consistent with *nothing to sweep* and is **not** evidence the sweeper works — that outcome is a **re-date, not a verdict**. Part (c) measures that directly. **Instrumentation:** no analytics event is involved and none is needed — the read keys on the two clock columns `V118` added, written by five verified call sites (four pool status writes, one note enqueue), so the metric is the table itself rather than an event that might not fire. **⚠️ EARLY READ RUN 2026-08-19 — the historical backlog DRAINED, which is NOT the same as the kill criterion passing, and the denominator clause above is what makes the difference.** Step 6a returned `FAILED/BOARD_EXAM 40` and `FAILED/LONG_EXAM 36` and **nothing else** — the query lists every non-`READY` status, so `GENERATING` = **0** and `PENDING` = **0**, down from the 37 that were stuck. That transition could not have come from normal use: recovery at `sampleQuestions:94` refuses to act while a row still reads `PENDING`/`GENERATING`, which was the defect. The 76 `FAILED` pools now self-heal through `refreshPool` on next use, which is the designed end state. **⚠️ But this is precisely the case the denominator clause anticipates:** it shows the sweeper fired at least once against a known backlog; it does **not** show it will catch a future stranding, because no deploy has interrupted generation since. **Part (c) still decides the verdict on 2026-09-01 — do not close this row on the early read.** **⚠️ Step 6a CANNOT attribute the change to the sweeper, and must not be quoted as if it does.** It selects `min/max(created_at)`, the clock `v0.86.0` itself declared unusable for pools because rows are reused — which is precisely why `V118` added `generation_status_at`. The `2026-07-03` timestamps therefore mean only *no new pool row has been inserted since then*, and a later reader could easily misread them as evidence the sweeper never fired. **Attribution query owed, one line:** `select generation_status, count(*), count(*) filter (where generation_status_at is null) as unstamped, min(generation_status_at), max(generation_status_at) from exam_question_pool where generation_status <> 'READY' group by 1;` — roughly 37 rows stamped at or after the `v0.86.0` deploy confirms the sweeper; all-`NULL` or all-pre-deploy means something else cleared them and the sweeper is still inert. **⚠️ Step 6b is still UNRUN, and it covers the more severe half** — the 1 Long Exam session stuck since 2026-05-19 was a *hard permanent block* (`LongExamService:169` hands the learner back the stuck session rather than creating one), where the pool damage only degraded exam starts to on-demand generation. **✅ VERDICT 2026-08-19 — PASS on the primary question, closed 18 days early.** Read (a) returned **0/0/0/0**: nothing past its bound on any of the four surfaces. Read (d) is what makes this a verdict rather than an absence: **76 FAILED pools, 39 unstamped, 37 stamped** — and **37 is exactly the stuck population** (18 `GENERATING` + 19 `PENDING`). `V118` seeded `generation_status_at` only for non-terminal rows, so those 37 *are* the previously-stuck set, and they are now all FAILED. Nothing but the sweeper transitions non-terminal → FAILED by age; recovery at `sampleQuestions:94` refuses to act on a non-terminal row, which was the whole defect. **This is what resolves the denominator clause** — there WAS a backlog, it was 37 rows, and it drained. Read (e) confirms the severe half: **the `LONG_EXAM` session stranded since 2026-05-19 is now `FAILED`**, so the hard permanent block is cleared. (The `IN_PROGRESS`/`LONG_EXAM` row from 2026-05-15 is an abandoned session, not a stuck one — `IN_PROGRESS` is deliberately not swept.) **⚠️ Residual, stated honestly: this proves the sweeper drained a KNOWN backlog once. It does not prove it catches a future stranding**, because no deploy has interrupted generation since. That residual is much weaker than the original question and does not justify holding the row open. **⚠️ The bounds half of this checkpoint is NOT answered and cannot be, until the stamp defect below is fixed** — see the `generation_status_at` row. | 2026-08-19 |

(2) `QuizShareLimitService.assertShareLinkQuotaNotExceeded` has **exactly one call site — link creation** — so a Free user pays the LLM cost for a 4th, 5th and 6th quiz before learning that none of them can be shared. **The cheaper limit is enforced last.** | **Disclosure only — no limit, counter or metering behaviour changes, and no second counter** (a separate meter for shared quizzes is a pricing decision nobody has taken). ⚠️ **Do NOT move the share-link check into the generation path**: generating without sharing is legitimate (teacher export, regenerate before sharing), so the cap must be surfaced earlier, not applied earlier. ⚠️ **The quota LABEL and the Challenge Quiz MODE name are different strings** — renaming the mode is out of scope and a global find-and-replace would destroy the distinction. | **~~No test ever executes a native query against PostgreSQL~~ — RESOLVED in `v0.93.0`, and it found TWO MORE production defects on its first run** | Found by a cold-context agent at the `v0.92.0` signoff, 2026-08-27, which `PREPARE`d all 31 native queries against a real PostgreSQL 16 carrying the full 125-migration schema | **RESOLVED 2026-08-28 (`v0.93.0` item 7).** `NativeQueryPostgresIntegrationTest` ships: one PostgreSQL 16 Testcontainers context applying all 125 Flyway migrations, a reflective `PREPARE` sweep over all 31 annotated native queries asserted at an exact count, execution of both PostgreSQL-only Library implementations, and assertions on the PG-only tag filter. Docker is required by default; the only opt-out is an explicit `-Dnativequery.pg.skip=true`, which warns. **⚠️ On its FIRST run it found two live production defects** — `wherelower(...)` from a Java text block stripping the trailing space before its closing delimiter, and `:learnerLeveland` from the next fragment starting at `and` — both PostgreSQL-only, both dating to `7f69f3d8` (2026-07-19), so **Public Library tag filtering had been broken in production for ~40 days**. **⚠️ Do NOT read this as total coverage:** `PREPARE` validates syntax, types and `ON CONFLICT` arbiter resolution and **never predicate correctness** — a cold agent mutated the `v0.93.0` conditional insert and the sweep passed it happily, which is why that statement now has its own executing test. `findNativeQueries` scans only `com.studysnap.backend.repository.**` via `getDeclaredMethods()`, and `appendSourceFilter`'s anonymous `viewerUserId == null` branches emit SQL the fixtures never produce. `NativeQueryParameterTypingTest` is retained as the Docker-free tripwire. Original analysis follows. **OPEN — this was the durable fix for the defect class that produced `v0.83.1` and the `v0.91.0` *Shared with you* 500.** **⚠️ Three structural facts, all verified: (1)** `src/test/resources/application.yaml` sets `flyway.enabled: false` and `ddl-auto: none`, so the **125 migrations never run in tests** — integration tests hand-write minimal H2 DDL per class (one creates `users` with TWO columns), which makes a `NOT NULL` column with no default **structurally undetectable**; that is `v0.83.1`'s root cause, still open. **(2)** `NoteLibraryRepositoryImpl` and `PublicLibraryRepositoryImpl` branch on `isPostgres()` at runtime, so **~20 dynamic queries have never executed on any database in any test** — their PG-only branches (`unnest`, `&&` array overlap, `cross join lateral`, `jsonb_array_length`) are unreachable by source-text or reflection because those strings exist only when assembled at runtime. **(3) There is no `.github/workflows` at all**, so every guard is honour-system on the local `./mvnw clean install`. **✅ Good news, empirically established: all 31 native queries currently PASS** — zero remaining instances of the class. `NativeQueryParameterTypingTest` was hardened at this signoff after the same agent proved it missed `:param is not null` (the identical failure, reintroduced and shipped green). **⚠️ Only TWO shapes actually fail on PG16** — a parameter whose sole type context is `IS [NOT] NULL`, and arithmetic on a bare parameter; `coalesce`, `nullif`, `case`, `lower()`, `$1 = $2`, `order by $1` and `limit $1` all prepare cleanly. **Do NOT lengthen the guard's pattern beyond those two** — that restores the guess `v0.85.0` argued against. **⚠️ Typing is ORDER-SENSITIVE:** `(col < :p or :p is null)` prepares while `(:p is null or col < :p)` does not, so "just reorder the OR" is a landmine a later readability edit re-arms — keep the casts | **Recommended and costed: `org.testcontainers:postgresql` + `junit-jupiter` (test scope) with `@ServiceConnection` and Flyway ENABLED so the real migrations build the schema**, carrying (a) a reflection-driven `PREPARE` sweep over every `@Query(nativeQuery=true)` — 31 queries, zero hand-maintenance, and the extraction pattern already exists in `LinkedLearnerInvitationReArmTest` — and (b) one integration test exercising every Library/PublicLibrary filter, the only thing that can reach the ~20 dynamic queries by forcing `isPostgres()` true. Cost: one test-scoped dependency, Docker at test time (already the documented dev loop), ~15–30s container start. **⚠️ The cheaper "PREPARE only when a PG is reachable" option was considered and rejected as false comfort** — with no CI it is honour-system either way, and it covers strictly less; enabling Flyway on real PostgreSQL is the half that closes `v0.83.1`'s class too | none — shipped | 2026-08-28 |

GPT core brief and Library surface module restamped to `v0.92.0` | **✅ DECIDED — scoped into the NEXT release, explicitly NOT folded into `v0.92.0`.** **Diagnosis: terminal signal AND distance, not terminal signal alone** — this corrects the session's working hypothesis, which had argued the stop-cue was the whole defect and that announcing the section would therefore be sufficient. | **Learning Connections — ratified five-phase direction (audit + plan)** | Owner-ratified product/UX direction, 2026-08-27, after an audit found two of the owner's four proposals already shipped, one that would corrupt a pre-committed measurement, and one blocked on data comparability. Plan: `docs/claude-plans/learning-connections-phase-plan.md`. Companion consultation prompt: `docs/claude-plans/linked-learners-surfacing-product-ux-consultation-prompt.md` | **Phase 1 shipped as `v0.91.0` — Shared Learning Material.** ⚠️ **The audit's headline finding stands independently of the plan: sharing a note meant PUBLISHING it**, and the only public surface mentioning supporter features still badges them *Coming Soon* with a waitlist button while `/help` is authentication-gated with no supporter section — **neither is engineering scope, and Phase 1 has no audience until both are fixed.** ⚠️ **`NoteVisibility` stays `PRIVATE \| PUBLIC`; a `SHARED` enum value is REJECTED** — access is owner-scoped in every read path so the enum grants nobody anything, and `AccountPurgeService` retains `PUBLIC` and deletes `PRIVATE`, so a `SHARED` note would match neither branch and survive the purge of a deleted account while staying readable. ⚠️ **Phases are SEQUENCED, NOT evidence-gated** (owner, 2026-08-27): with zero connections in production a gating read returns nothing, so gating would be an indefinite stop. ⚠️ **Authorization is moving from role-directional to permission-directional** — `requireAcceptedLearnerId` hardcodes caller-is-supporter, which Phase 3 replaces with per-direction, per-scope grants; guardian consent must be re-asserted inside that check or `v0.89.1`'s gate quietly reopens. | **Phases 1 and 2 are RELEASED** (`v0.91.0`, `v0.92.0`, both signed off 2026-08-27): `note_shares` and controlled material sharing, then the `linked_learner_grants` table with **both** scopes but using only `ACTIVITY`, a `requireGrant` check with guardian consent re-asserted inside it, directional opt-in UI, and a momentum view. **Phases 1, 2 and 3 are RELEASED** (`v0.91.0`, `v0.92.0`, `v0.93.0`). Phase 3 shipped 2026-08-28: it reads the `PROGRESS` scope `V125` already ships, reimplements `requireAcceptedLearnerId` over `requireGrant(..., PROGRESS)`, adds the per-scope permission UI and a progress grant→view funnel — **one migration, not two, as planned; this release adds none.** **⚠️ Re-verified against code at the `v0.93.0` kickoff, and it is why Phase 3 was chosen over any other candidate:** `LinkedLearnerProgressService.getProgress` gates on `requireAcceptedLearnerId` alone — no grant check — and returns `getStudyEngagement(learnerUserId)` verbatim, **the identical four fields Phase 2 put behind a grant**, plus a superset, so the Phase 2 activity toggle does not bound what a supporter sees. **⚠️ The reimplementation's trap, recorded here so it is not rediscovered:** `requireGrant` returns the *other party* — the **supporter** when the caller is a learner — so a thin passthrough would silently ship bidirectional progress; §4's per-direction table describes the grant **row**, not the endpoint, and **the read stays unidirectional**. **⚠️ After `v0.93.0`, an `ACCEPTED` relationship no longer implies progress access**, and **no backfill grants `PROGRESS` to existing accepted relationships** — free while production is empty, forbidden anyway because it converts an implicit rule into explicit consent nobody gave. **Phase 4 is RELEASED but PARTIAL** (`v0.94.0`, 2026-08-29): shareable single-use invitation links and connection management shipped; **supporter onboarding did NOT**, because onboarding was frozen until `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]`. That freeze lifts imminently, making supporter onboarding the natural `v0.95.0` — it is the only named Phase 4 item still outstanding. **⚠️ Link redemption creates `PENDING` and the CREATOR confirms**, because with a link the inviter names nobody and redemption alone would be one party agreeing twice; do not 'simplify' that to direct acceptance. **⚠️ Carried into Phase 5's decision, not a defect:** an unconfirmed redemption permanently writes the redeemer's write-once birth year and discloses their name and minor-status to the creator — widened from the email path, which required already knowing an address. **Phase 5 (motivation experiments) remains uncommitted; Phase 5 (motivation experiments) remains uncommitted** — rings, leaderboards and reactions each need their own decision. **⚠️ Carried into Phase 4 as the top recorded limitation:** `LinkedLearnerProgressResponse.engagement` is byte-for-byte the payload the `ACTIVITY` scope gates, so a live `PROGRESS` grant makes the activity toggle INERT, and the learner is not told — found independently by two cold agents from opposite directions at the `v0.93.0` pressure test. It is a disclosure defect, not an access one, and the honest fix (couple the toggles, or drop `engagement` from the progress payload and require both grants) is a product decision `v0.93.0`'s own anti-drift rule forbade it from taking. Neither Phase 2 nor Phase 3 adds any measurement: `MEANINGFUL_STUDY_ACTIVITIES` already defines what *studied* means (excluding `OPENED_STUDY_PACK`), and Phase 3 permissions an existing payload without adding a field. | none — Phase 4 is sequenced, not gated | none — Phase 5 is uncommitted, and supporter onboarding unblocks at 2026-09-11 | 2026-08-29 |
| **Support Another Learner — helping someone else learn, as a capability rather than a Profile Type** | Owner direction ratified 2026-08-14, revising a same-day "Guardian profile" draft that modelled it as a new `ProfileType`. Proposal: `docs/claude-plans/support-another-learner-proposal.md`. Surfacing ratification: `docs/claude-plans/learning-companion-surfacing-product-ux-consultation-prompt.md` (written and answered 2026-08-20 — **a standing decision a future re-proposal must read, not a spent artifact**) | **Direction ratified, scope NOT ratified.** The job is *help someone else learn* — a parent, grandparent, sibling, tutor, mentor or coach — not one relationship. **`ProfileType` answers "how do YOU learn?"; supporting someone answers "who else are you helping?"** Those stay independent axes, the same correction `ADR-001` made for `course_program`; a supporter may therefore also be a learner, which an enum value forbids. **Ratified rollout is three phases:** (1) help another learner — no shared subscription, no shared quota, no dashboards, **no permissions model**; (2) linked learners, invitation and acceptance; (3) progress sharing. **Phase 3 is where the product's first cross-user read lives**, so Phases 1–2 ship without touching authorization at all. **Ratified privacy line: a supporter sees readiness, progress and quiz performance, NEVER the learner's personal notes** — notes are personal thinking, progress is educational, and the line also protects the core loop, since learners who suspect notes are visible write less honestly. **Ratified: no per-learner charge** — the link is metadata and costs nothing; each learner keeps their own account and quota | **✅ SURFACING RATIFIED 2026-08-20, after a product-UX consultation was written and the owner answered it directly.** **Option A (an opt-in toggle) REJECTED — Learning Connections stays a CAPABILITY, never a profile mode or an opt-in mode**; a preference would re-introduce the very mode the capability framing exists to remove. **"Quiz for someone" leaves the Study Pack practice row entirely** — it is a support/share action, not learner practice, and does not belong beside Start Quick Review, where it also risks becoming an avoidance path on the surface retention is decided. It moves to a secondary **note action / share** surface. **⚠️ CORRECTION TO THE SESSION'S OWN PROPOSAL: quiz sharing is NOT gated on having an accepted connection.** That option coupled two independent things — **a shared-quiz recipient needs no account and no relationship**, so sharing cannot depend on one existing. **Accepted connections gate supporter-specific surfaces ONLY** (People You Support, progress); they never determine whether sharing exists. **Learning Connections stays independently discoverable**, so someone with no relationship can still learn about both. **`/progress` stays focused on the CURRENT learner**; supporter progress stays under Learning Connections / Dashboard, because the two carry different privacy contracts. **⚠️ Connection invitations stay ONE-AT-A-TIME as a principle, not a deferral: sharing a quiz link IS the many-recipient mechanism, while forming an ongoing, permission-bearing relationship is personal and should stay deliberate.** The email-keyed-invitation work still stands on its own merits — it closes the account-existence oracle (**SHIPPED in `v0.90.0`**, along with invitation expiry, two-key rate limiting on the invite endpoint, and a verified-email gate on every path that grants or widens access; relationship rows are now created only at acceptance, which changed what `PENDING` means — see the `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-19]` row, whose read this alters) regardless of whether multi-recipient invites are ever built. **✅ Q4 AND Q5 ALSO RATIFIED 2026-08-19, which unblocks Phase 2 scoping.** **Q4 — linking is invite + accept in both directions, revocable from either side.** Acceptance is the load-bearing half: an opt-out notification would leave the relationship existing, and progress potentially visible, before the learner agreed to anything. Learner-initiated-code-only was rejected — strictest, but it requires the learner to act first, failing exactly where the parent→young-child case needs it most. **Q5 — age is collected AT LINK TIME, never at signup, with a guardian consent step below the threshold.** Collecting at link time keeps onboarding untouched, which matters concretely because `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]` is measuring it against a 62.4% baseline. **⚠️ `learnerLevel` was considered as an age proxy and REJECTED** — it implies minors for `GRADE_SCHOOL`/`JUNIOR_HIGH`/`SENIOR_HIGH` and costs nothing, but the mapping breaks both ways (`PERSONAL_LEARNING`, adult re-takers) and a proxy is weaker ground than a declared age. Adults-only-first was rejected because it excludes the motivating parent→child case. **⚠️ THE AGE THRESHOLD ITSELF IS NOT DECIDED AND MUST NOT BE GUESSED IN SCOPING** — the mechanism is ratified, the number is a legal question under the Philippine Data Privacy Act and is owner-owned pending counsel. Build the threshold as configuration, not a literal, and ship no default that reads as a legal position. **✅ THE DECISION GATE IS CLEARED — audit run 2026-08-19, and Phase 1 SCOPED INTO `v0.89.0`.** The answer to *does Phase 1 already exist* is **yes, almost entirely**: generating a quiz for another person is ungated, `/quiz/share/**` is `permitAll` so the recipient needs no account, and plan metering already exists. **One gate hides all of it — `QuizShareLinkService.requireTeacherOrAdmin:160-166`** — and it is an axis error, not a missing feature: a parent helping a child is a `STUDENT`/`BOARD_EXAM` profile and must misrepresent their own profile to unlock it. **⚠️ The audit also DISSOLVED three of this row's other blockers for Phase 1 specifically**, because Phase 1 records no relationship: consent has nothing to consent to, minors/DPA store nothing about the recipient, and *whose quota* is already answered by the existing code (the supporter's own account and own share limit). **They all return from Phase 2**, when a relationship is recorded. Remaining original gate text follows. **`[DECISION]` + `[EVIDENCE]`.** **Decision first: does Phase 1 already largely exist?** Teachers already generate quizzes for others via `generatedQuiz`, and shareable quiz links already work — so Phase 1 may be a **packaging and discoverability** problem rather than a build. Audit that before scoping. **Also undecided: whose quota a supporter-initiated generation consumes** (the link is free, but generating costs tokens — recommendation is the supporter's own, which dissolves the pricing question entirely), the consent model for linking (**must be mutual, or anyone could claim a relationship by knowing an email**), and minors/Data-Privacy-Act obligations, since age is not collected today. **Evidence: zero users on the unimplemented `PARENT` enum value.** A demand signal is designed in §9 of the proposal — **and it must NOT go in onboarding before 2026-09-11**, which is under measurement against a 62.4% baseline. **Do not justify Phase 1 on retention:** the supporter's return loop is the progress view, which is Phase 3 | 2026-08-14 |
| **Adaptive Practice becomes the recommendation engine, not a post-quiz next step** | Owner direction ratified 2026-08-14. Full direction, findings and open questions: `docs/claude-plans/adaptive-practice-recommendation-engine.md` | **FIRST SLICE SHIPPED as `v0.77.0` (2026-08-14):** the Dashboard's weak-concept focus now resolves from `ConceptHealth`'s `incorrect_streak` against the existing `TWICE_MISSED_STREAK_THRESHOLD` instead of the single latest completed Quick Review, so one bad quiz no longer produces a recommendation. **Within-pack only** — cross-pack still needs canonical concept identity. **No entry point was removed**, so the 2026-09-12 constraint is untouched. Philosophy ratified, remaining scope NOT ratified. Adaptive Practice should appear because a learner has demonstrated a **persistent** weakness across multiple assessments — not because they just finished a quiz. Primarily **dashboard-driven** (Today's Focus / Weak Concepts), with Challenge Quiz completion celebrating the quiz instead of offering remediation. Target feeling: *"the system noticed something I keep struggling with"*, not *"the system wants me to take another quiz"*. **This is the first mechanism that would make "Always know what to learn next" literally true.** Smaller than it sounds in one respect: the Dashboard already routes to Adaptive Practice from its due- and weak-concept branches, so the change is **raising the evidence bar and making it the primary route**, not adding a surface | **`[DECISION]` — and it carries a HARD SEQUENCING CONSTRAINT.** ⚠️ **Do not remove the Challenge Quiz entry point before 2026-09-12.** `v0.74.0` already removed Adaptive Practice from the Quick Review result screen, and the open `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-12]` measures exactly that; a second removal confounds the read rather than answering it. **Worse, that checkpoint's kill criterion says to "restore a demoted entry point" if adoption falls — which directly contradicts this direction**, so the checkpoint ~~must be re-specified now (remedy becomes *accelerate the dashboard recommendation*), or it will fire in September and someone will correctly follow an instruction that undoes a ratified direction.~~ **✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-14 — the remedy WAS re-specified, and the checkpoint row carries it struck through. ⚠️ THIS WARNING IS STALE AND IS STRUCK THROUGH RATHER THAN DELETED, because it caused a REAL wrong scoping decision at the `v0.107.0` kickoff (2026-09-03), which scoped an already-completed re-specification as new work.** Two rows described one obligation — this one carried the warning, the checkpoint row carried the completed action — and the warning row was read first. **A future reader needs the discharge visible HERE, on the row that misled, not only on the row that was right.** **Architecture finding: the missing layer is CONCEPT IDENTITY, not just a learning signal.** `ConceptHealthEntity` already stores `incorrect_streak`, so within-pack repeated evidence is **available today** — but `concept` is a free-text string keyed per Study Pack, so cross-pack recommendation (*"this keeps coming back everywhere"*) needs canonical concepts first, the same problem `ADR-001` solved for programs. **Within-pack and cross-pack differ by roughly an order of magnitude in cost; decide which is meant first.** **Second fork:** this either justifies consolidating the **8 independent "what's next" resolvers** the Companion Guidance Doctrine pressure test found, or becomes the 9th — and that doctrine's blocking Phase 0 (Primary-Review-Set vs Study/Exam-Focus) probably cannot be dodged by a dashboard-first engine. **Product tension:** Adaptive Practice is capped at 3/month on Free, so a higher evidence bar may mean the tier most likely to convert never sees a recommendation at all | 2026-08-14 |
| **Onboarding Intent Router — onboarding as an intelligent router, not profile collection** | `v0.71.0` pre-signoff pressure test (`docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-pre-signoff-pressure-test.md`), which found onboarding the flow most affected by ADR-001 Release B. Full plan: `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-activation-and-intent-router.md`. Production audit queries: `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-review-set-vocabulary-audit.sql` | **Scoped into `v0.71.0` as slice 5 — owner ruling 2026-08-06.** Added after kickoff, deliberately. Trigger was **B0**, an activation-blocking regression this release introduced: `resolveRequestedCourseProgram` gained a `throw` where `main` returned null, while onboarding creates its first note without sending a program — so a new user gets *"Choose at least one course or program."* on a screen with no such field and cannot complete onboarding. **Owner-ratified governing philosophy: onboarding is no longer profile collection — it is an intelligent router that helps every learner reach the fastest successful study experience available to them.** This generalizes Phase 1's practice-first branch (below) from a `BOARD_EXAM` carve-out to every profile type, with a first-intent step whose copy resolves against the learner's own program *before* selection, and an honest availability state for unsupported programs | **GATE FULLY CLEARED 2026-08-06 — the production audit ran and came back clean.** All four published Official Review Sets are tagged with **exact catalog names** (Accountancy, Architecture, Education, Nursing); no exam slugs, no case drift, no mixed conventions, so **no resolution layer is needed** and building one would be the unnecessary normalization the audit was commissioned to avoid. The Intent Router is unblocked. Results: `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-review-set-vocabulary-audit-results.md`. **Two findings from the audit that change the design weighting:** (a) coverage is far better than the program count suggests — the four covered programs hold **179 of 218 program-holding accounts (82.1%)**, so Branch A serves most users and the "Coming soon" state is a ~18% minority path, correcting an earlier reading that argued from 4-of-21 programs; (b) **38.7% of accounts (141/364) still never complete onboarding**, closely tracking the ~40% recorded 2026-07-28 by an independent path — this is the pre-change baseline to re-measure against, and the audit also confirmed the fire-and-forget context write has really bitten users (5 completed with no course program, 4 with no learner level). Residual, not blocking: production tagging is clean but unguarded — the admin publish picker draws options from public **note** values rather than the catalog, so the recommended slice-5 guard is to repoint that picker, not to normalize the match path. Why the owner overrode deferral: onboarding is a one-time flow, so unlike slice 4 there is no re-learning cost — the decision rests on not shipping a first impression that models the wrong product. **Slice 5 shipped in `v0.71.0`; C8 and C9 did NOT close and were re-recorded at signoff 2026-08-10** per the rule in `RELEASES.md` that they be re-recorded rather than closed if slice 5 did not cover them. **C9's stated blocker expired but the finding is untouched:** slice 5 moved `profileType` to Step 1, so Step 2 now knows it is talking to a curator, yet its Course / Program copy is still byte-identical for all four profile types — a teacher is still asked for a free-text program that can never be a note program under the two-mode model. It should be re-triaged on effort, not carried as blocked. **C8 is half-fixed, and the remaining half is what C8 was actually about:** C5 stopped the editor contradicting itself (no more *"Tailored for: X"* over zero selected programs), but §3's authoring-time affordance did not ship — a curator whose profile program is off-catalog still gets bare *"No course programs selected."* with no explanation, asserted by a passing test in `note-editor-page-client.test.tsx`. **Considered and NOT taken as `v0.72.1`'s build half, 2026-08-11 — and the reason matters, because it is not a demotion.** The activation read measured the cost of this surface precisely: **132 users, 35.2% of all signups**, verify their email and then never complete onboarding, making it by far the largest single leak in the funnel. It was not taken because recovering that leak is *not the fastest path to returning users* — activation is already 52.2%, which structurally caps the volume lever below the retention lever — **not** because the findings are weak or the cost is small. **Do not re-read this row as deprioritized on merit.** What the read removed is one specific justification: these can no longer be scoped *as a retention intervention*. They remain fully live on their own terms. **Also recorded: the read could not locate where *within* onboarding the 132 drop** — `profile_type` is only persisted at onboarding's final step, so the `users` table cannot distinguish a step-1 drop from a step-4 one, and the event-based read has n=5. Anyone scoping this should expect to answer that first | 2026-08-11 |
| **`v0.71.1` — four architectural deferrals from the `v0.71.0` signoff pressure test** | `docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-signoff-pressure-test.md` — **⚠️ PROPOSED FOR DELETION AT THE `v0.97.0` KICKOFF AND KEPT, 2026-08-29.** The four deferrals did all ship, but the file also carries **Frontend #4, explicitly deferred and still carried**: the deferred-completion marker is never retried, `completeOnboardingAndLeave` is a new call site that increases exposure, and **that exit is the only terminal one that does not `clearOnboardingDraft`**. Production sizing returned **0** for the admin-authored shape and **no** non-admin-owned note carrying any join row, so prevention was free and cost nothing; no cleanup migration. Ships with the deliberately asymmetric admin-or-owner **read** path preserved and an owner-scoped Admin Dashboard. **(2) and its folded (4) shipped 2026-08-10** (PR #1019). **Do not treat `V108` as having closed these classes**, and **CLOSED at `v0.71.1` signoff, 2026-08-11** — all four items are fixed, none was re-recorded, and the pressure test still owes it a whole-release read since items 1 and 2 both landed in `NoteApplicableProgramsService` | 2026-08-11 |

| **One canonical Course / Program catalog — proposed `ADR-001` amendment removing learner free-text** | `docs/claude-plans/course-program-canonical-catalog-proposal.md`, raised by the owner 2026-08-11 while scoping `v0.71.2` | **PROPOSED, not ratified.** Would amend `ADR-001`'s two-mode model so learners select from the catalog rather than typing, with a *Request Program* action where theirs is missing. **The audit found the premise is half-true and the harm is narrower than stated:** four of the six "core dimensions" are already catalog-only, and the real leak is `NoteService.listPublicCoursePrograms:1213-1221`, which unions legacy free-text from *public* notes into the public filter vocabulary. Measured: **4,480** private learner notes rely on free text against a **21-row** catalog, while only **4** non-admin public notes exist — so it constrains 4,480 notes to clean a surface currently four notes wide. **Counter-proposal recorded:** return catalog names only from the public vocabulary and make suggestions catalog-first, which fixes the verified harm in one method with no migration **✅ EVIDENCE HALF ANSWERED 2026-08-19 — and it says DECLINE, on grounds neither the proposal nor the query anticipated.** Read: `docs/claude-plans/course-program-fragmentation-read.sql`. **The amendment's premise has essentially no production footprint: learners have written 4,737 PRIVATE notes and FOUR public ones, zero of them off-catalog.** The thing it exists to prevent — learner free-text fragmenting community content — is four notes. **The off-catalog population is not drift, and it is not learners':** of 63 off-catalog notes, **48 are curator-authored**, and **42 (67%) are academic LEVELS used as programs** — `Junior High` (24), `High School` (15), `Grade School` (3). That is a category error, not free-text drift; **locking the field would not fix it, the author would simply pick a wrong program instead.** The genuine remainder is ~14 notes wanting catalog entries (`Civil Service` 7, `Software Engineering` 4, `Engineering` 2, `Biology` 1) — and `v0.71.2` already made adding those possible **without a migration**, which is the cheaper answer the amendment would foreclose. Plus `Professional / Board Exam Review` (7, all private), the known study-context catch-all. **⚠️ The pre-committed normaliser hypothesis was FALSIFIED, not confirmed:** part 5 returned **zero** casing/whitespace near-misses, so a normaliser has nothing to normalise either. Accounts sit at 33/234 = **14.1% off-catalog against `v0.79.0`'s 13.9% baseline — unchanged**, exactly as predicted for the deliberate onboarding exclusion, and therefore still not evidence for locking. **The actionable finding is a DIFFERENT row: academic levels as Course / Program**, now sized at 42 notes, 37 of them public and reachable in discovery.  | **`[DECISION]` + `[EVIDENCE]`.** Decision: is prospective community-content fragmentation worth a migration now, given Trust → Habit → Community puts that content last? Evidence that would settle it: a production read of distinct `course_program` values on public notes and across accounts. **⚠️ THAT READ IS NOW WRITTEN AND UNRUN — `docs/claude-plans/course-program-fragmentation-read.sql` (2026-08-19).** Five parts, smoke-tested for syntax. **It is built to argue AGAINST itself:** it splits curator from learner notes (900+ curator notes would otherwise drown the signal), splits public from private (only public mints a discovery facet), and part 5 isolates values that are off-catalog *only* by casing or spacing — **if those are most of the off-catalog population, the answer is a normaliser, not a locked field, and this amendment should stay declined.** Part 3 also re-surfaces academic levels used as programs, which is its own row. **⚠️ A high off-catalog rate on ACCOUNTS is expected and is NOT evidence for locking** — it is the known, deliberate cost of excluding onboarding from `v0.79.0`, itself gated on `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]`. **Two traps if taken:** `resolveRequestedCourseProgram` currently throws when both note and profile values are null, so every learner off the 21-row catalog would be unable to save their next edit; and at a curator headcount of one, a blocking *Request Program* makes note creation depend on the owner's response time — the B0 shape, slower. **Not `v0.71.2`** — that release is correct under either outcome | 2026-08-19 |

Creating e.g. a *Health Sciences* family before any medical notes are authored is the same premature expansion `ADR-001:74` rejects for programs: a family that expands to 5 programs when the usual case is 2 costs more clicks than it saves, since the author then trims three | **`[EFFORT]`, demand-gated.** The trigger is an author noticing they have selected the same program set several times running — not a taxonomy judgement that two programs feel related. **Two constraints for whoever builds it, recorded so they are not rediscovered:** (1) the family picker must read `program_families` **directly** rather than deriving distinct families from programs the way `admin-course-program-catalog-section.tsx:27-31` does today, or a newly created family is invisible until something already uses it — you could not assign the family you just made; (2) **membership is the real lever, not creation** — `ADR-001` says *"if a family should expand to a different set, that is a change to membership"*, so the surface needs add/remove-member, otherwise you get families you cannot correct | 2026-08-11 |

| **L9 — `notes.status` nullability differs across test schemas sharing a JVM-lifetime H2** | `docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-pre-signoff-pressure-test.md:639`; carried from `v0.71.0` Known Limitations | **Not taken in `v0.71.1`, with a reason — added 2026-08-11 when the pressure test found it had no disposition anywhere.** It was the only one of the twenty carried Medium/Low findings the triage never mentioned in either direction, and the Backlog row for that set was about to close declaring all twenty handled. `notes.status` is `not null` in three test schemas and nullable in two, over a shared H2 using `create table if not exists`, so the first schema to load wins and the others silently inherit it | **`[EFFORT]`** — the fix is consolidating the schemas behind one definition, which changes how every integration test boots. | **Overlapping representations on a learner's copy — the bug class `v0.71.1` patches the symptom of** | `ADR-001` → *"No learner-facing Applicable Programs UI" governs authoring, not provenance display* (ratified 2026-08-10), which closes the symptom and explicitly leaves this open | **Deferred at the `v0.71.1` learner-visible-programs ruling, 2026-08-10.** A curated note copied by a learner carries **both** representations — `notes.course_program` "as copied" plus inherited join rows — while read semantics make the join rows win whenever they exist. The string is therefore unreadable on that shape, and `v0.71.1` fixes that by no longer requiring it and by showing the inherited programs read-only. **That is symptom treatment: the two representations still overlap, and every future surface reading either one inherits the ambiguity.** Two options close the class outright — **(D)** stop inheriting join rows on copy, so a learner's copy becomes a personal-string note; **(C)** let a learner's own value clear the inherited rows on their copy. **Sharpest evidence for eventually taking one:** `canManageVisibility` lets a learner flip their copy to `PUBLIC`, and public discovery is join-first, so inherited curator rows can drive **public** shelves for a note the curator never published. Default-private is not stays-private | **`[DECISION]`** — both options amend ratified `ADR-001` text (D contradicts the canonical-shapes table's third row; C is the learner-save synchronization the representation-authority ruling rejected, though that rejection's stated reason — cannot distinguish derived from inherited rows — does not apply on a copy, where every row is inherited by construction). **Not a patch-release decision**; do not fold into `v0.71.1`. Un-parked by an owner call on whether a learner's copy should keep curator applicability at all, which is a product question about what a copy *is*, not a data question | 2026-08-10 |

| **Canonical Knowledge Architecture (Applicable Programs / Domain Context / Note Learner Level)** | Owner + product-UX GPT discussion relayed 2026-08-03, prompted by hitting the single-valued `course_program` wall while expanding the Official Library into Civil Engineering. Full critique, code audit (59 backend + 40 frontend non-test files), 14-item migration inventory, and 4-step sequencing in `docs/claude-prompt/canonical-knowledge-architecture-out/01-architecture-critique-and-migration-plan.md`; draft ADR in `docs/architecture/ADR-001-canonical-knowledge-architecture.md`; production vocabulary audit queries in `03`/`04` with results and revisions in `05-vocabulary-results.md`; proposed Domain Context taxonomy in `06-domain-context-taxonomy.md`; product-UX-GPT consultation prompt in `07`; **Query K validation + answers to the seven governance questions in `08-taxonomy-validation-and-final-recommendation.md` — read `06` through `08`, which invalidates part of it** | **Planned, not ratified — decomposed into 4 independently-gated steps.** Audit found the direction right and the code evidence stronger than the proposal's own: `OpenAiLlmStudyPackService:1536-1537`'s "treat the course/program above as the authoritative academic domain … Do not blend in material from unrelated disciplines" is logically unsatisfiable under many-valued programs, making Domain Context forced rather than optional. Three corrections to the proposal: (1) it is 3 initiatives with non-comparable cost/reversibility, and **Domain Context alone unblocks canonical authoring** because Review Sets already compose notes freely by explicit reference — so comprehensive Official Review Sets do not wait on the join table; (2) the migration premise is wrong — there is no `course_programs` table (`V39__note_course_program.sql` adds a plain `VARCHAR(120)` column despite its name), `UpsertNoteRequest.courseProgram` has no validation, and an LLM suggestion can write into the field (`StudyPackService:746`), so the backfill is vocabulary reconciliation with editorial judgment calls, not `INSERT … SELECT`; (3) Note Learner Level *reverses* an explicit current rule (`:1540-1541` "Do not use learner level to calibrate static note or Study Pack content" — static depth is set by course/program today and learner level is deliberately excluded) and requires re-keying `exam_question_pool.learner_level` + `challenge_quiz_question_bank.learner_level`, both of which key on **user** level today via `ExamQuestionPoolService.sameLearnerLevel()`. Also surfaced, none of it in the proposal: `notes.target_profile_type` (`NoteTargetProfileType`, NOT NULL) is an undiscussed fourth axis — checked, and it is **not** write-only: it is a live Public Library audience filter (`PublicLibraryRepositoryImpl:176-178`, `NoteController:594`/`:636`, `NoteRepository:106`/`:131`), so it sits on the *discovery* axis alongside Applicable Programs rather than conflicting with Note Learner Level; the real question it raises is whether precise program facets make a coarse 3-value audience facet redundant, judged at the end of step 3, not now; `getExamSlugForCourseProgram` returns exactly one slug via `.find()`, so many-valued programs make Exam Hub resolution config-order-dependent; `isQuantitativeContext` would be diluted by a joined program list; Review Set course/program is behavioral (`NoteCollectionService:175` public filter, `:1231` hierarchy cascade) and can now legitimately diverge from its notes; personal copies must not inherit Applicable Programs (`NoteService:264`). **Step 3 fails bootstrap-test clause 2** (not withdrawable without a migration once filters/badges read the join) — recorded as a knowing multi-release commitment, not an oversight. Does **not** supersede Company Redefinition Phase 3b: this ADR makes knowledge canonical, not generated artifacts — pools stay per-`study_pack_id` | **[DECISION] — FULLY RATIFIED 2026-08-03. No gates and no open decisions remain; Release A is ready to `/kickoff`.** Ratified in a second pass the same day: **(1)** the **8-value Domain Context set** — `Engineering Mathematics`, `Engineering Sciences`, `Civil Engineering`, `Professional Practice & Regulation`, `General Education`, `Professional Education`, `Nursing`, `Accountancy`; **(2)** **`Architecture` deliberately gets NO Domain Context** despite 837 notes — it uses the program-name fallback until shared canonical knowledge accumulates, because duplication reduction and not note volume is what earns a context (the clearest worked example of the governance rule); **(3)** the axis is renamed **Content Context → Domain Context** (`notes.domain_context`) before the migration — it names the field's actual job (feeding the prompt's "authoritative academic **domain**" line) and avoids a semantic collision with the pre-existing `OpenAiLlmStudyPackService.buildContentContextBlock` (`:1523`), which already means the static-content variant of the context block; **(4)** the success metric is restated as *"Eliminates duplication of shared knowledge as NoteLib expands to more programs"* — comprehensive Review Sets, faster expansion, and lower maintenance are downstream benefits, not the guarantee; **(5)** governance now states that **Domain Contexts are expected to remain relatively stable and adding one is an architectural decision, not routine curriculum authoring** ("adding notes is authoring; adding a Domain Context is architecture"). **The ADR is Accepted and promoted to `docs/architecture/ADR-001-canonical-knowledge-architecture.md`** — the repo's first ADR, establishing the `docs/architecture/ADR-NNN-*.md` convention. Superseded history: the one decision this row previously listed as open was **the Domain Context value set** — proposed in `06-domain-context-taxonomy.md` (10 values: Tier 1 `Engineering Mathematics` / `Engineering Sciences` / `Civil Engineering` to unblock the Algebra work, Tier 2 covering existing content, Tier 3 explicitly not minted). Its rule — *Domain Context is the coarsest label under which the note's treatment is identical* — rules out the original proposal's "Engineering Foundation"-style values as **weaker domain constraints than the `Civil Engineering` they replace** (risk R4), since the value is substituted into the prompt's "treat the course/program above as the authoritative academic domain" line. `06` §6 carries five owner questions, the load-bearing one being whether `Engineering Sciences` is shared by all 11 engineering programs or a subset (a PRC syllabus question, not an architecture one — the subject groupings in `06` Tier 1 are **unverified against current board syllabi** and must be curator-checked, with `06` §7's Query K provided for that). **All data gates are closed.** Production vocabulary audit (`03`) and follow-ups (`04`) both run 2026-08-03; results in `05-vocabulary-results.md`. Every gate this row originally carried is now closed by data: **(a) Step 2's cost gate cleared cheaply** — 27 note-side / 16 user-side values, **32 in the union, ZERO character-level collisions**, so the feared typo-variant reconciliation does not exist and Step 2 folds into Step 1's release (~6 semantic judgment calls remain: `Bsed`→Education, the Computer Science/IT/Software Engineering overlap, `Engineering` as a family, `Biology` as a subject, `Civil Service`/`Professional / Board Exam Review`/`Self Study` as activities). **(b) Step 3's **RETIRED CHECKPOINT** (was due 2027-02-01 — deliberately written without the live-checkpoint token so the kickoff step-9 scan does not flag it) was answered affirmatively before it was needed.** **The motivating case is anticipatory, and should be quoted as the reason rather than the corroborating find below:** under the Civil Engineering Review Set's **Engineering Mathematics** subject plan, the next authoring step is creating topic notes for the **Algebra** subject; the owner stopped before creating them, because those same notes will be needed by ten further engineering programs and authoring under a single-program model commits to duplicating them ten times. The decision is "do not create the duplication in the first place," taken at the moment of authoring. **Corroborating evidence, not the reason:** Query J found duplication has independently already begun — `Strength of Materials` holds "Stress and Strain in Strength of Materials" (Civil Engineering) and "Stress, Strain, and Material Strength" (Mechanical Engineering), the same knowledge as two notes under two programs, **invisible to Baseline A's exact-title matching**. Baseline A's 0.00% must never be cited as evidence of no duplication. Nine further CE SoM notes are queued to need a twin per additional engineering program. **(c) The "maybe assembly is the real gap" reading is WITHDRAWN as wrong** — the owner confirmed the CE Review Set is mid-build and blocked by precisely this; Query I corroborates (197 CE official public notes: 94 across 4 PRIVATE collections, 1 public, 102 in none). **Steps 1+2+4 should ship as ONE release, not three**: all additive/reversible, all touching `buildGenerationContextBlock`, and **49 notes currently feed a K-12 grade level (`Junior High`/`High School`/`Grade School`/`Senior High – STEM`/`– ABM`/`– HUMSS`) into the prompt's "treat the course/program above as the authoritative academic domain" line — an active generation-quality bug today**, in the same method Step 1 already edits; its backfill is mechanical for those six values, and all 49 are in zero collections so the risk is low. Pool/bank re-keying is the one separable PR inside that release. Step 3 follows as its own multi-release effort, now justified on evidence rather than held as a bet. **§9 Baseline B restated from Query H's hierarchy rollup: 4 comprehensive Official Review Sets averaging ~58 notes (CPALE 74, PNLE 63, ALE 52, LET 43) against a target of several hundred** — the earlier "avg 8.6" was per-subject-child and must not be quoted. Two adjacent items surfaced and are explicitly NOT folded in, each needing its own row: **Bulk Generate intra-program de-duplication** (near-duplicate titles within one program, e.g. "High Alert Medications in Nursing Pharmacology" vs "…in Nursing Practice") and the **general assembly backlog** (~330 official public notes across 5 programs in no collection) | 2026-08-04 |
