All five required properties hold: dates, kill criteria, this column, the review ritual, and instrumentation shipped in the same release (`REVIEW_COMMITMENT_PROMPT_SHOWN` / `_COMMITTED` / `_DECLINED`, `DUE_CONCEPTS_DIGEST_LANDED`, `DUE_CONCEPTS_DIGEST_FIRST_ANSWER_SUBMITTED`). **Verified emitting, not merely declared** — enum membership is not instrumentation, and this table's own rule is that a checkpoint without a working metric is decorative. Each of the five has a real fire site with a test: the three commitment events at `review-commitment-prompt.tsx:64` and `:102`, both digest events at `quick-review/page.tsx:250` and `:264`. **⚠️ `v0.74.0` item 5 puts a small dent in this, flagged at that kickoff 2026-08-12:** *Review the Notes* replaces *Finish Review* on the incorrect-answer screen and navigates away after completing the session, so a learner whose **first-ever completed session** is a Quick Review with at least one miss never reaches the results screen where `ReviewCommitmentPrompt` renders — the commitment device is not offered and `REVIEW_COMMITMENT_PROMPT_SHOWN` does not fire. Narrow but reachable (a first-timer missing a question is ordinary), and it shrinks this checkpoint's denominator rather than biasing it. **The mitigation costs nothing** — place *Review the Notes* on the results screen instead of replacing the pre-completion button. Raise it once before implementing item 5; the lifecycle ruling was made before this was known. **Why two tiers rather than one 30-day retention read:** a single W1→W2 re-check at 30 days walks straight back into the read that just fired — ~31 activated users/month means n≈31 again and P(zero \\| no change) = 54.3%, failing bootstrap clause 3 outright and reproducing exactly the decorative-checkpoint failure the `v0.48.0` digest-lift postmortem records. **Proximal (2026-09-10) — the funnel this release actually instrumented,** whose denominator is every learner finishing a first session and is therefore readable at current scale: `PROMPT_SHOWN → COMMITTED` rate, and `DIGEST_LANDED → FIRST_ANSWER_SUBMITTED` rate among committers. **Kill criterion:** if learners do not commit when asked, or committers do not act on the digest they asked for, the retention question never arises — the return-loop framing reverts to **unconfirmed** and is reopened rather than iterated on with further nudge tuning. **Distal (2026-11-09) — RE-SPECIFIED 2026-08-11, before it could fire against a broken instrument.** It originally read "the W1→W2 read itself." `v0.72.1`'s Query 5 then measured that the days 7–14 window sees only **3 of 11 real returners (27%)** — 4 return in days 2–7 before the window opens, 4 only after day 14. Left as written, this checkpoint would have fired in November measuring H1+H5 through an instrument that misses 73% of returns, and would likely have reported it ineffective regardless of what H1+H5 actually did. That is the decorative-checkpoint failure in its most damaging form: not a missing metric, but a confidently wrong one. **It is therefore now specified as the returned-after-day-7-unbounded read** (any activity after day 7, no upper bound), reported **alongside** the strict days 7–14 figure so the two definitions stay visible against each other and neither can be quoted alone. Cut by the exam-date segmentation defined in the Target-habit row below rather than as one blended percentage. **Denominator clause:** if the sample is still underpowered at that date, record "not yet measurable" and re-date it — that is not a verdict, and the proximal tier is what carries the kill criterion precisely so this one does not have to. **Dates and criteria proposed by Claude Code 2026-08-11, owner may override** — same precedent as the Challenge Quiz adoption row | 2026-08-11 |
| **Target-habit definition** (exam-date-relative return cadence vs. the current calendar-week W1→W2 boolean) | This session, 2026-07-28 — surfaced during a strategic reprioritization discussion (see "Prioritization Lens & Strategic Frame" above and the updated "Product Learning Loop" section at the bottom of this doc) | **Defined 2026-07-28 — retires the single blended W1→W2 boolean as the universal yardstick.** Segment by whether `UserEntity.examDate` is set (not by `profile_type` — a coarser proxy for the same thing; some non-`BOARD_EXAM` accounts may also set a real exam date). **(1) Exam-bound learners** (majority — `BOARD_EXAM` alone is 70.94% of profile-typed accounts, see the population-mix row above): natural arc is signup → sustained practice → sit the exam → legitimately stop. Metric: among users whose exam date has already passed, did they have ≥1 qualifying practice session in the 7 days immediately before that date? Users whose exam date hasn't arrived yet are `in_flight` — excluded from the scored denominator entirely (same convention the Diagnostic Read already uses for cohorts whose 14-day window hasn't closed), not scored as success or failure either way. Going quiet only *after* a user's own exam date is not churn — that's expected completion. **(2) Open-ended learners** (`STUDENT` and any account with no exam date set, ~27%+): no single terminal event — the existing W1→W2-style windowed read remains a reasonable frame here specifically; refine later against the learner's own `studyDaysPerWeek`, not urgent. **Does not excuse the existing 0/41 exam-dated-retention finding** — that measures disengagement *before* the goal, a real problem under either frame; the reframe only stops penalizing expected disengagement *after* the exam date. **Small-n caveat:** the scored group (exam date already passed) is likely very small right now — recent signups, PRC-clustered exam dates — a near-single-digit denominator means "not yet measurable," not a verdict, the same trap Round 1 already caught itself in with Query 2's 0/39. **Post-exam users still matter for other purposes** (referrals, retakes, Knowledge Impact creator supply) — not counting their post-exam quiet as retention failure doesn't mean they're uninteresting. **Application:** Round 2 (Diagnostic Read + H1+H5, due ~2026-08-06) should re-cut by this segmentation rather than one blended percentage — the query is a small job once run against real data then, not written now | **PARTIALLY ACTED ON in `v0.72.1`, 2026-08-11 — recorded here so this row does not read as untouched.** This row retired the blended W1→W2 boolean as the universal yardstick in *definition*; `v0.72.1` was the first release to change anything in *code* on the strength of it. Query 5 measured why it mattered: the days 7–14 boolean sees **3 of 11 real returners (27%)**, missing everyone who returns in days 2–7 or after day 14. The admin funnel now reports four windows side by side, with the strict figure preserved unchanged for comparability. **What this row still owes, and what was deliberately left:** the **exam-date segmentation** is NOT implemented — widening the window addresses the open-ended-learner half this row already called reasonable, while the exam-bound metric it defines (practice in the 7 days before a passed exam date) remains unbuilt, and this row's own small-n caveat still applies to it. Un-parked by that scored group becoming large enough to read | 2026-08-11 |
| Profile-type population mix across the full user base (STUDENT vs. BOARD_EXAM vs. other `ProfileType` values — not just the surge cohort) | Same reprioritization discussion, 2026-07-28 — needed to check whether "Review Set adoption is displacing note-authoring as the primary populate-path" holds product-wide or only for the `BOARD_EXAM`-only practice-first fast-path | **Run 2026-07-28 — answered, caveat does not apply.** `15-profile-type-population-mix.sql`, run against production: of profile-typed accounts, `BOARD_EXAM` is 70.94% (144) vs. `STUDENT` 27.09% (55) (Query B); `PROFESSIONAL`/`TEACHER` negligible. Not a surge artifact — pre-surge accounts were already 63.3%/34.0% `BOARD_EXAM`/`STUDENT` among profile-typed users (Query C), and the surge then pushed the post-surge window to 92.5%/7.5%. `BOARD_EXAM` has been the dominant segment since before the surge, not a surge-only skew — the `BOARD_EXAM`-only practice-first adopt-a-Review-Set fast-path reaches the majority segment, not a minority carve-out, so "Review Sets are becoming the real product" does not need the STUDENT-dominance caveat this row originally flagged. **Separate finding, not this row's original question but worth logging:** 40.1% of all accounts (136/339, Query A) and 27.4% of the surge-and-after window (20/73, Query C) have `profile_type` still NULL — corroborates, via an independent measurement path, the Diagnostic Read's existing ~50% onboarding non-completion finding (see the Diagnostic Read section above). A large minority of signups may never reach a profile-typed state at all, which bears on how the target-habit / retention reads above should be interpreted — a return-cadence habit can't be built by users who never finish onboarding in the first place | none — answered | 2026-07-28 |
| **`bg-card` Tailwind utility resolved to no-op** — 23 literal usages across 9 files (22 rendered elements) rendered as bordered-but-unfilled panels instead of the elevated-surface fill the code implies | Found 2026-07-30 while pressure-testing `v0.66.0`'s `ScoreReveal`-nesting concern (a `bg-card`-on-`bg-surface-alt` "card-in-a-card" worry that turned out to depend on whether `bg-card` even resolves) | **Shipped — v0.66.2.** No `--color-card`/`--card` token existed anywhere in `frontend/app/globals.css`'s `@theme inline` block, so Tailwind v4 silently dropped the unrecognized utility rather than erroring. `--card` registered aliased to `--background` (not `--surface-alt`) — chosen and verified via actual `getComputedStyle` checks, not just visual screenshot inspection: standalone nested panels (`ScoreReveal`, `public-flashcards-preview.tsx`) sit inside a parent `Card` (`bg-surface-alt`) and genuinely gain visible separation from `--background` (`rgb(255,255,255)` vs. the container's `rgb(249,250,251)`, confirmed by computed style, not eyeballed). Count corrected twice before shipping: 9 → 21 (missed `long-exam.tsx` ×8 and `challenge-quiz.tsx` ×5 on first re-check) → 23 literal occurrences / 9 files / 22 elements on a stricter recount (a Codex audit found the flashcard flip-button contributes 2 source-line occurrences for 1 rendered element). That same Codex audit also caught a real reasoning error: two call sites (`challenge-quiz/page.tsx:2090`, `long-exam/page.tsx:1090`) apply `bg-card` directly on a `Card` component alongside `Card`'s own default `bg-surface-alt`; the original scoping claimed this was a deliberate override the fix "preserved," but `cn()` has no `tailwind-merge` and the compiled stylesheet's cascade order (not JSX class order) decides equal-specificity ties, so `bg-surface-alt` wins regardless — confirmed via `getComputedStyle` returning an identical color for both an "override" `Card` and a plain `Card`. The token choice itself was unaffected (it rests on the nested-panel evidence, not the false override claim), but the claim was removed from `RELEASES.md` rather than left standing. One incidentally-verified genuine visual change: `quiz-generation-overlay.tsx`'s modal panel goes from a translucent blur-bleed-through card to a solid, legible dialog — checked in both themes, no regression | none — shipped | 2026-07-30 |
| **Messaging Architecture — "We sell the learning system, features support the promise"** | Owner + Claude Code discussion, 2026-07-31/2026-08-01, originating from Company Redefinition Phase 4 §4 item 4 (Pro exam-pass re-messaging) — widened after the initial Board-Exam-Mode-led drafts were reconsidered as narrowing NoteLib's identity back to an exam-review app. **Supersedes the "External marketing copy vs. internal 'learning OS' positioning" row this replaces** (2026-07-28) — that row's narrower landing-page-copy concern and its `[DECISION, then EVIDENCE]` conversion-test gate are both absorbed by this wider initiative | **Ratified 2026-08-01.** Core principle: *"We sell the learning system. Features simply support that promise."* (Spotify-Premium analogy: sell "Listen without interruptions," not offline playlists/bitrate/skips — NoteLib should sell "Always know what to learn next," not Board Exam Mode/Companion/Adaptive Practice/Review Sets/Progress individually). Product-wide scope, not a one-off paywall rewrite: landing page, pricing page, upgrade flows/paywall modals, checkout, marketing copy, and in-app upsells (e.g. Exam Hub, quota-limit modals) should all eventually reinforce the same hero and hierarchy rather than reinventing framing per surface; this principle extends to future product copy generally, not only monetization surfaces. **Hierarchy (locked):** Hero (universal emotional outcome, profile-agnostic) → Supporting paragraph (explains "the learning system," still profile-agnostic) → Profile-specific bullets (personalizes via the existing `ProfileType`-keyed copy-resolution pattern already used elsewhere in this codebase — e.g. `getCollectionLabels`, per-plan `adaptivePracticeMessage` — not a new mechanism) → Features (evidence supporting the promise; this is where Board Exam Mode, Companion, Adaptive Practice, Review Sets, Progress, Knowledge Impact, Explore each live — none of them become the hero anywhere). **Ratified hero/supporting:** "Always know what to learn next." / "NoteLib turns your notes into a complete learning system — organized, prioritized, and ready whenever you sit down to study." **Illustrative profile bullets (to be refined at implementation scoping, not final copy):** Student — stay organized across every subject · know what to study next · practice with confidence. Exam Taker — stay organized across your review · know what deserves attention before exam day · practice under real board-exam conditions with Board Exam Mode. Teacher — organize teaching materials into ready-to-use Review Sets · build better assessments faster · track your students' progress. Professional — **flagged aspirational, not shippable as real copy yet**: `PROFESSIONAL` is enum-only today with no dedicated feature set (per `CLAUDE.md`); illustrative bullets ("keep growing after graduation, one note at a time," "refresh what matters most before it's needed," "stay ready for certifications and recurring requirements") describe an experience that doesn't exist yet — do not wire this profile's bullets live until Professional has real capability behind it. **Plus/Pro naming:** product names stay unchanged everywhere (checkout, Settings, badges, receipts, support) — avoids the bifurcated-vocabulary risk this project already identified and avoided one release earlier, when Creator/Curated Learning labels were deliberately kept internal-frame-only rather than user-facing (Company Redefinition Phase 4, ratified 2026-07-31). Only the aspirational tagline changes: `plans.ts`'s existing `PRO.title` field (currently "Best for exam prep") and `PLUS`'s equivalent become outcome-framed — e.g. Plus → "Guided learning built around your notes," Pro → "Your complete learning system" — `name: "Pro"` / `name: "Plus"` untouched. **Deliberately not gated behind a pre-launch conversion experiment** — an explicit owner call distinguishing this from pricing/checkout *mechanics* changes (which still need evidence): narrative-consistency-with-product-vision is treated as a product design decision, not an optimization experiment. Conversion is still measured post-launch and iterated on if it regresses; it just doesn't block initial rollout. **Second slice shipped as `v0.76.0` (2026-08-14) — the money surfaces.** `FREE.title` (the item this row named as explicitly owed) is now *"Start with ready-made study material"*, the `PLAN_COMPARISON_ROWS` "Best for" row describes learner situations, and the paywall headline/body carry the promise. **Two rulings from that slice bind future ones:** upgrade **button** labels stay feature-named (a button says what the click does), and `PLAN_CARD_SUBTEXT` must describe the tier rather than a feature, because it is keyed on plan type alone and renders on every paywall. `FREE.description` was deliberately left factual. **Still un-actioned: the landing page, the Exam Hub upsell, per-plan feature bullets, and both `TEACHER_*` paywalls** — the last of these needs a teacher-positioning scoping pass that has not happened. **First slice shipped in `v0.68.0` (2026-08-01)** — this row was written before that merged and originally read "Not yet implemented anywhere." The `/pricing` hero and supporting paragraph shipped the ratified copy **word for word** ("Always know what to learn next." / "NoteLib turns your notes into a complete learning system — organized, prioritized, and ready whenever you sit down to study."), along with the `<meta description>`, the second-section heading, and the exact `PLUS`/`PRO` taglines this row ratifies ("Guided learning built around your notes" / "Your complete learning system"), with `name: "Plus"`/`name: "Pro"` untouched as specified. `FREE.description` was also rebalanced at signoff — for card-alignment reasons, not positioning, and deliberately kept a factual feature list so it makes no tier-boundary claim against the ratified `FREE=adopt` placement. **`FREE.title` is explicitly still owed to this initiative:** an outcome-framed candidate was written during `v0.68.0` and reverted, because deriving it from consistency with `PLUS`/`PRO` alone contradicted `FREE=adopt` — it needs writing against the hierarchy above, not against its siblings. **Every remaining surface is unimplemented** (paywall modal, Exam Hub upsell, landing page, `PLAN_COMPARISON_ROWS`'s "Best for" row, per-plan feature bullets) | **No gate on the ratified architecture** (owner decision, not awaiting evidence) — and explicitly **not** gated on a pre-launch conversion experiment, per the owner call recorded in Status. Each remaining surface needs its own scoping pass and its own `/kickoff` before code changes; this is explicitly incremental, not a single release. `FREE.title` is the one already-identified concrete item, and must be written against the locked hierarchy rather than derived from `PLUS`/`PRO` consistency | 2026-08-01 |
| **`ONBOARDING_V2_ABANDONED` instrumentation bug** (over-fires on every onboarding step transition; several early-return redirects leak the event with no matching `ONBOARDING_V2_STARTED`) | Found investigating the "Funnel re-check" anomaly above (`ABANDONED` distinct-users 69 > `STARTED` distinct-users 68) — root cause verified by direct code read (`frontend/app/onboarding/page.tsx`) plus an independent Opus pressure-test that read the same code and refined the analysis (found the leak-path mechanism the initial over-fire theory alone didn't explain) | **~~In progress — sent to Codex 2026-07-28~~ — LANDED, verified in code 2026-08-19.** Both bugs are fixed in `app/onboarding/page.tsx`: the abandonment cleanup now carries `[]` deps with a `currentStepRef` mirror (so it fires on true unmount only, not on every step transition), and the shared invariant `!startedTrackedRef.current \|\| !shouldTrackAbandonmentRef.current` guards the emit, which is the single-guard shape the prompt specified rather than the per-site patching that caused the bug. **⚠️ The row sat at "in progress" for three weeks after the work shipped** — second stale row found in the same 2026-08-19 pass. **Still genuinely open:** real abandonment (tab close / hard navigation) is still never captured — no `pagehide`/`beforeunload`/`visibilitychange` handler exists — and the discriminating query `16-abandoned-leak-path-check.sql` is still unrun. Original text follows. **In progress — fix scoped, sent to Codex 2026-07-28.** Prompt: `docs/codex-prompts/v0.61.0-onboarding-abandoned-event-fix.md`. Two compounding bugs: (1) the abandonment cleanup is keyed on `[draft.currentStep]`, so it fires on nearly every step transition, not just true unmount; (2) several early-return redirects (already-verified-email, two already-completed-onboarding checks, a `getMe()` rejection) never clear the abandonment guard and never reach `STARTED` either, leaking `ABANDONED` with no matching `STARTED`. Fix: decouple the cleanup from `draft.currentStep` via a ref, and add one shared invariant (`!startedTrackedRef.current \|\| !shouldTrackAbandonmentRef.current`) rather than patching each leak site — the per-site approach is what produced the bug (two adjacent branches on the same condition, only one clearing the guard). **Separately, real abandonment (tab close/hard navigation) is currently never captured at all** — no `pagehide`/`beforeunload`/`visibilitychange` handler exists; that's a distinct, larger, not-yet-scoped gap, intentionally out of scope for this fix. Discriminating query available, not yet run: `16-abandoned-leak-path-check.sql` | Codex delivery, then Claude Code audit-diff before commit (per this doc's standard Codex-delivery process) | 2026-07-28 |
| **Authoring by inference — evolve the note authoring UX from manual classification to inferred metadata with explicit override** | Owner reframing 2026-08-04, after pressure-testing the "should a note have a Learner Level?" question with a product-UX GPT (`14-learner-level-necessity-gpt-consultation.md`). **Recorded as a direction in `ADR-001` rather than only here**, because it constrains what any future authoring surface may do | **SHIPPED as `v0.75.0`, signed off 2026-08-14.** Depth only — Domain Context stays uninferred, and the `Authored Depth` rename shipped copy-only with the column unchanged. **One binding narrowing found at the pressure test: the profile pre-fill applies to CURATORS ONLY.** It initially fired for every profile while the control renders for Teacher/Admin alone, so learners silently persisted an invisible, unclearable depth — constraint (b)'s safety argument is that a human *saw* the value, which a hidden field cannot satisfy. Learners therefore get no depth pre-fill and keep resolving through the profile fallback. **Still open from this row, deliberately: the Review Set leg fires only on bulk-generate**, because that is where bulk authoring happens; single-note creation still has no Review Set context. **The four constraints below remain binding on any future authoring surface.** Historical direction follows. The four-axis knowledge model is unchanged and confirmed: depth is a property of the *content*, since Grade School / Senior High / College Engineering / Board Review Algebra are different knowledge artifacts rather than one artifact with four quiz settings. Two placements were considered and rejected on the record — depth on the *reader* alone (makes a College-profile learner get a college quiz on a grade-school note) and depth on the *program* via `course_programs.learner_level_id` (Civil Engineering spans Year 1–4 plus Board Review, Nursing spans College plus Board Review, so one program has many depths and normalising just relocates the coupling). What changes is authoring: **Review Set → author profile → explicit override.** Four constraints are binding and recorded in the ADR: **(a)** Subject and Domain Context are **not** valid depth-inference sources — `Algebra` spans every depth, and inferring depth from Domain Context re-couples the two axes the release just separated; **(b)** inference is a **UI pre-fill only, never a server-side default write**, because `domain_context IS NULL` is the promotion-backlog marker and a default would silently destroy it; **(c)** year-level granularity (CE Year 1–4) is **out of scope** — `COLLEGE` stays a single value; **(d)** the admin label may be renamed (`Educational Level` / `Authored Depth`) but **not** to `Intended Audience`, which `notes.target_profile_type` already occupies in the editor | **GATE FULLY CLEARED — verified at the `v0.75.0` kickoff, 2026-08-13, and KICKED OFF as `v0.75.0` the same day.** Both sequenced prerequisites were already satisfied, and this row had gone stale in a way that made unblocked work look blocked. **First prerequisite — "make authoring metadata editable on `STUDY_PACK_READY` notes" — SHIPPED IN `v0.70.0`, four releases before this row was last reviewed.** The claim that *"the editor closes once a Study Pack exists"* is false: `AGENTS.md:1081` records that on a `STUDY_PACK_READY` note, Edit stays on Note Detail and Teacher/Admin authors may edit Target Audience, Domain Context, and Note Learner Level; `private-note-detail-page-client.tsx:1445-1452` opens that inline metadata editor for any non-draft note. **Second prerequisite — R4 — RESOLVED 2026-08-04** (`ADR-001:266`), passing on all three steps. **The same staleness sits in `ADR-001`'s own "Sequencing — gated on R4" subsection and is corrected there in this release (item 6), which matters more:** an ADR outranks a feature doc, so a stale gate inside one blocks work that is actually unblocked. **Scope note carried into the release: DEPTH ONLY.** `ADR-001` constraint 1 authorizes an inference chain for `learner_level` alone and names **no** source for Domain Context, which is also the field where a pre-fill is most dangerous — `domain_context IS NULL` is the promotion-backlog marker, so a pre-filled select a curator tabs past silently converts an observable backlog row into a decided one. **Leg 1 needs building, not just wiring:** `NoteCollectionEntity` carries no `learnerLevel`, no plan-template entity carries one, and no authoring surface knows its target Review Set — so the ADR's "a CE Board Review set is board-depth by construction" is a reading of a title, not a stored fact | 2026-08-13 |
| **R4 — does a broader Domain Context degrade generated content?** (post-deploy verification owed by `v0.69.0`) | `01` risk R4, restated in `RELEASES.md` v0.69.0 under "Verification owed." The concern: a Domain Context is often *broader* than the `course_program` it replaces (`Engineering Mathematics` vs `Civil Engineering`), and a vaguer domain constraint can make generated content drift generic. No test detects this — the prompt-building tests assert which values reach the model, never whether the output is good | **RESOLVED 2026-08-04 — passed on all three steps, run against production once `v0.70.0` deployed.** The 8-value Domain Context set is **not** amended. Control regenerated near-identically; level precedence held (a `BOARD_EXAM_REVIEW` note read by a `COLLEGE` profile produced board-level multi-step items). Runbook and rubric: `17-r4-verification-runbook.md`. Recorded in ADR-001 under “R4 verification” | **Gate cleared. Bulk authoring is unblocked** — the constraint that survived three releases is satisfied, not merely restated. **Does NOT settle applicability**: R4 validated the Domain Context *value set*, not which programs share which subjects. The `06`/`08` groupings remain unverified against current PRC board syllabi and are a `[DECISION]`/`[EFFORT]` gate on Release B’s family-expansion defaults | 2026-08-04 |

Most likely variance on optional summary blocks | **[EVIDENCE]** reproduce across several regenerations before theorising. **⚠️ ATTEMPTED AND DEFERRED 2026-08-19, NOT TESTED AND NOT KILLED.** `v0.89.0` was kicked off against this defect and rescoped the same day: item 1 was the reproduction, and it needs a human to create a probe note, run ~8 live regenerations and read the results — owner time that was not available. **Nothing was learned; this row loses no strength.** **⚠️ Do NOT read the rescope as evidence that regeneration is fine** — that would be the opposite of what happened, and it is exactly the misreading a future reader is most likely to make from a release that opened here and shipped elsewhere. **The harness is written and preserved: `docs/claude-plans/v0.89.0-regeneration-reproduction.md`** — the endpoint is traced (`POST /notes/{id}/generate`, the same one as first generation, permitted by `resolveSourceNoteForGeneration(..., true)`), the probe content is drafted so both optional blocks unambiguously apply, the scoring is scripted, and the kill criterion is pre-committed. **Next attempt starts by running it, not by re-deriving it.** ⚠️ Run it LOCALLY, never against the published note — regeneration is in-place with no version history, so the probe would inflict the very defect under test on real material. User-facing stake is real and independent of R4: a learner regenerates and loses content they valued, with no warning and no version history to recover from | 2026-08-04 |
| **Topic-note Quick Recall validation rejects valid formula content** — `Create from topic` fails intermittently with `LLM_INVALID_OUTPUT` on formula-heavy material | Reproduced and documented in `docs/claude-prompt/topic-note-quick-recall-validation-review.md`, logged 2026-08-03 from a real failed request (Course/Program `Civil Engineering`, subject `Hydraulic Structures`, topic `Weirs`). The brief replayed the repo prompt, schema, and configured free model against OpenAI: four of five sampled responses passed, one failed — output-dependent, not deterministic | **Primary issue FIXED in `v0.69.0`.** Two output contracts disagreed: the JSON schema allowed 240 characters per generated-note item while backend validation rejected any item over 28 whitespace-delimited words, and the prompt never mentioned the word ceiling. The rejected item was a correct discharge formula with its variables defined — 199 characters (valid) but 38 "words" (invalid). A whitespace word count measures the wrong thing on notation. Fix: `quickRecall` is now bounded by characters against the same constant the schema uses, `coreDetails` and `whyItMatters` keep the 28-word prose ceiling, and the prompt states the bound so the model is no longer judged against a rule it was never told. **This mattered on the critical path** — v0.69.0 exists to unblock authoring Engineering Mathematics topic notes across eleven engineering programs, which are formula-heavy by definition, so bulk authoring would have hit this intermittently. **Still open: the secondary finding.** `GenerateNoteFromTopicRequest` carries `topic`, `courseProgram`, and (since PR 3) `domainContext`, but **no `subject`** — `NoteGenerationService` builds its context with `subject = null`, so a note authored under subject `Hydraulic Structures` never tells the model that. It degrades quality rather than failing requests, and any fix must preserve ADR-001's hierarchy: Domain Context sets the authoritative domain, Subject only narrows within it | **[EFFORT]** — for the remaining subject-contract question only. Needs a scoping pass on whether `subject` joins the topic-generation contract and how it composes with Domain Context; not data-blocked | 2026-08-04 |
| **Note editor silently submits the editing user's own profile Course / Program onto a note whose `course_program` is null** — the field renders empty while a different value is sent | Found 2026-08-03 while scoping `v0.69.0` PR 4, checking whether ADR-001's "clear `course_program` once the level moves out of it" step was safe to execute. Root cause verified by direct code read of `frontend/components/notes/note-editor-page-client.tsx`, not inferred from the feature doc | **~~Open, not scoped~~ — RESOLVED, and it had been resolved for 16 days before anyone noticed.** Fixed by `c34b6116` (2026-08-03), shipped in `v0.69.0`. `note-editor-page-client.tsx` now resolves `isEditMode ? normalizeOptional(draft.courseProgram) : …`, so edit mode reads the note's own value only and a missing one falls through to the validation prompt rather than being silently classified. **⚠️ This row's `Last reviewed` was 2026-08-04 — one day AFTER the fix — and it survived six releases still reading "Open, not scoped".** Recorded because the review ritual bumps `Last reviewed` without re-reading a row's Status against code, so a shipped item can keep advertising itself as a candidate indefinitely. Found 2026-08-19 while picking the next release; it was the leading candidate until the code disproved the row. Original text follows. **Open, not scoped.** `:269` calls `setProfileCourseProgram(me.courseProgram ?? "")` **unconditionally** — the `isEditMode` guard at `:270` gates only the *draft* prefill, not the profile state it reads from. So when editing a note with a null `course_program`, `:592`'s `normalizeOptional(draft.courseProgram) ?? normalizeOptional(profileCourseProgram)` resolves to the **editor's own profile program**, and `:620` writes it into the update payload — while the visible Course / Program input stays empty, because the draft was deliberately not prefilled in edit mode. The user sees a blank field and saves a value they never chose, with no toast and no validation stop (`:611` only fires when the resolved value is *also* empty). Live today for any null-program note; 26 such notes in the local dev dataset, production count not taken. **This is why ADR-001's second corollary defers the clearing step entirely** — PR 4 would have created 38 more of these on official public notes, so the ADR declines to clear rather than making the backfill depend on this being fixed first. The fix is small but carries a judgment call this row does not pre-empt: either drop the profile fallback in edit mode (create-only prefill, which is what `:270` already implies was intended), or keep it and make it honest by prefilling the draft so the submitted value is the displayed one. The second preserves current behavior; the first matches ADR-001's direction of travel, since a profile program silently entering note metadata is precisely the free-text contamination the ADR exists to remove. **RESOLVED 2026-08-03 — owner chose the first: drop the profile fallback in edit mode.** Profile context may pre-fill during creation only; it must not populate or alter metadata when editing an existing note. A note with no course/program now hits the normal validation prompt, which is the intended failure mode ("ask the author to classify the note", not "silently write profile metadata onto the note"). Shipped in `v0.69.0` with a regression test asserting `updateNote` is not called and the profile value is not submitted; verified to fail without the fix. Topic generation still uses the profile program as a *generation input* only, never persisted, and is deliberately unchanged | none — shipped | 2026-08-04 |
| **Challenge Quiz adoption — the three validation reads deferred in June 2026** | `docs/product/CHALLENGE_QUIZ_ADOPTION.md`, restored 2026-08-03 from the superseded Facebook group playbook. The decision shipped its product half (post-session next-step promotes `Take a Challenge` at >= 4/5, not only 5/5) and explicitly said *"validate later, don't pre-optimize"* — naming three reads, none of which this repo records as having been run | **Open — shipped ahead of evidence, obligation never closed.** The decision's load-bearing claim is that low Challenge Quiz adoption is a **value-is-unclear (motivation) problem, not a button-placement problem**, and that framing is what ruled out moving or enlarging the entry point. It has never been tested. **Instrumentation audited 2026-08-03, and the three reads do not have equal standing:** (a) **Quick Review → Challenge conversion, before vs. after the 5/5 → 4/5 change — MEASURABLE NOW.** `CHALLENGE_QUIZ_STARTED` (added 2026-03-23) and `QUICK_REVIEW_COMPLETED` (added 2026-05-05) both predate June 2026, so a genuine before window exists in the analytics table; the before window is fixed and closed, so this read does not improve by waiting, it only accumulates denominator. (b) **Return rate of converted users — MEASURABLE NOW**, derivable from `PUBLIC_NOTE_COPY_CLICKED` plus session data; needs a query, not new events. (c) **Challenge CTA impressions vs. clicks — NOT MEASURABLE.** No impression event and no click event exists for the post-session Challenge CTA; `CHALLENGE_QUIZ_STARTED` cannot separate *seen-and-ignored* from *never-reached*, which is the entire discrimination. **This is the read that would falsify the decision's own framing, and it is exactly the "checkpoint without a working metric is decorative" failure this table's own gate-type rules name** (see the v0.48.0 digest-lift postmortem). Closing it needs two events shipped first — a post-session Challenge CTA impression and a click — which is a small, self-contained piece of work and a prerequisite, not part of the read | **~~[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-30]~~ — READS (a) AND (b) RUN 2026-08-13 AGAINST PRODUCTION. KILL CRITERION MET: *"motivation, not placement"* reverts to UNCONFIRMED.** **The numbers.** Conversion per completed Quick Review, before (2026-05-19→06-14, n=34) vs after (2026-06-16→08-12, n=132) the 5/5→4/5 change: **24h attribution 41.2% → 44.7% (+3.5pp, +0.37 SE); 7d attribution 58.8% → 47.0% (−11.9pp, −1.25 SE).** **Neither difference is distinguishable from zero, and that is the finding.** **The two windows disagreeing in SIGN does not trigger this file's "opposite directions → inconclusive" rule** — that rule exists to stop someone picking the flattering window, and it is not engaged when neither direction is significant. A sign flip at these sample sizes is what noise looks like, so it is confirmatory of no effect rather than grounds to withhold a verdict. Recording "inconclusive" here would leave the row open forever and guarantee nobody re-runs it, which is precisely the failure this row was opened to document. **The population shift makes the negative read STRONGER, not weaker — do not dismiss this as "cohorts were not comparable".** Sanity read (a3): sessions reaching the `>= 4/5` promotion went **68.6% → 92.0%** (avg score 78.9 → 91.1, learners 11 → 79). Far more sessions actually SAW the promotion, and conversion still did not rise. A population that is more eligible for the lever and converts no better is a harder negative than the raw rates suggest. **Read (b), return rate of converted learners, is directionally consistent and evidentially weak:** 20.0% → 11.0%, and 13.3% → 9.6% on the 14-day-observable restriction. **Numerators are 2–9. It corroborates; it must not carry an argument on its own.** **Data-quality note:** 2.9% (before) and 3.6% (after) of completed sessions have no `QUICK_REVIEW_COMPLETED` event — client-fired analytics lost on navigation. Both are well under the effect sizes and change nothing. **What follows, and what does NOT.** The ineffective change is **already reverted**: `v0.74.0` item 4 moved the promotion back from `>= 4/5` to verified mastery, so nothing needs building in response. The claim that low Challenge adoption is a **motivation** problem rather than a **placement** problem — the framing that ruled out moving or enlarging the entry point — is now **UNCONFIRMED and should be reopened**, not patched with further promotion tweaks. **THIS ROW STAYS OPEN.** Per its own rule, (a) and (b) measure whether the fix worked; only (c) tests why. **Read (c) is measurable for the first time** — `v0.74.0` shipped its prerequisite `POST_SESSION_CHALLENGE_CTA_IMPRESSION` and `_CLICKED` events, which is what separates *seen-and-ignored* from *never-reached*. **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-15]` for read (c)** — deploy+60 from the `v0.74.0` deploy, reset at signoff if the deploy date moves; 60 days rather than 30 because impressions accrue at roughly the Quick Review completion rate (~2.4/day over the after window) and a click-through read needs a few hundred impressions to say anything. **Kill criterion for (c):** if impressions are high and clicks are low, adoption is a **motivation** problem and the original framing is vindicated; if impressions are low, it is a **reach/placement** problem and the entry point must be reconsidered. **Denominator clause applies unchanged** — too few impressions at the due date is itself the finding, not grounds to extend. | 2026-08-13 |

Pressure test found the *taxonomy* ("one learning responsibility per feature") sound but the *literal system-merge* too wide — "Companion" today names three structurally different things (admin-authored static content, learner-reactive derived guidance i.e. "Coach," and the LLM chat), and merging the name removes the vocabulary keeping them safely apart (a learner would see "Companion" guidance on Dashboard, then be told it's unavailable on a Review Set with no admin-authored content). Also found 8 existing, independent "what's next" resolvers already exist across Dashboard/Collection detail/Progress (each with documented reasons for diverging, e.g. Dashboard/collection pacing must stay uncoupled per `docs/features/dashboard.md:95`) sitting on only 2 shared signal primitives — a real, costly merge target, not free. **Resolution: no rename, no new user-facing brand, no backend merge now.** Adopt "one learning responsibility per feature" + "one question per surface" + a de-duplication rule as an authoring *doctrine* (docs/copy only); keep `Feature.ASK_COMPANION`/`ask_companion_sessions`/`AnalyticsEventType.ASK_COMPANION_*` unchanged. Phased plan if this is ever scoped: Phase 0 resolve Primary-vs-Focus (blocking) → Phase 1 doctrine + copy audit (cheap, no backend) → Phase 2 additive-only structured `reason` field on existing resolvers (substrate, no merge) → Phase 3 lightweight one-off "why this?" LLM explainer (needs a new message-unit mechanism — cannot reuse `ask_companion_sessions`'s session-unit quota/DB-CHECK turn cap) → Phase 4 Personalization (unchanged). Merge the 8 resolvers only if Phase 1 empirically proves the ladder is identical across surfaces — explicit go/no-go criterion, recorded here so it isn't re-litigated. | Knowledge Impact (creator-recognition dashboard — "your notes helped N learners") | `docs/claude-prompt/company-redefinition-out/09-knowledge-impact.md`, sourced from a GPT-authored roadmap-addition proposal relayed 2026-07-25 | **Ratified by owner 2026-07-28, kicked off same day as `v0.62.0` — see this doc's own "v0.62.0 — Knowledge Impact" section above.** Proceeding despite the data gate below, kept as history not overridden by fiat. Owner's reasoning: near-zero community publishing likely exists *because* creators get nothing back for it — the chicken-and-egg counter `09`'s own analysis already named as a live, un-resolved branch ("this could be a small lever on the publishing rate, not only a reward waiting for scale"), not a new argument being waved through. Weighed against months of flat retention with no lever having moved it yet, owner chose to try this rather than keep waiting on more data. **On record, not a blocker:** `09`'s own "Answers to the memo's 12 questions" §11 frames this as a creator/ecosystem feature with at best a second-order retention effect, explicitly *not* a lever on the general ~2.4% W1→W2 problem the Diagnostic Read (due ~2026-08-06) is the actual read on — worth having visible given retention is the stated motivation here, but the owner's call stands regardless. **Checkpoint committed 2026-07-28 (strategic reprioritization discussion):** treat this as a bet with a re-check, not an indefinite ship-and-forget — re-run the gate query (`14-knowledge-impact-gate-check.sql` or its successor) 30-60 days after this ships, and treat a continued-zero or still-collapsed community-publish rate as settling the "can we create creators" hypothesis for good, not a reason to keep waiting. Worth being explicit that the only trend data available *right now* (monthly community-publish rate 1→3→0 across May/June/July, zero in the highest-signup month on record) argues against the "dormant creators waiting for a reason to publish" story rather than being neutral — this ratification is a deliberate bet against the most recent data point, not a bet made in an information vacuum. See the "Creator Loop" entry in the updated "Product Learning Loop" section at the bottom of this doc. Prior gate-query history, superseded by this ratification: **Parked, data-gated. Gate query run 2026-07-28 — failed, more concentrated than the original inference, not less.** `docs/claude-prompt/company-redefinition-out/14-knowledge-impact-gate-check.sql` (wider Java-level official-author definition — email-or-admin-role, not the narrower SQL predicate, see that file's own inconsistency note): 3 distinct non-official public-note creators (threshold was order-of-magnitude 20-30+); 697 official public notes vs. 4 community (99.4% official); views/copies split 12,835/2,994 official vs. 231/2 community (~1.8%/~0.07% community share); monthly community-publish rate 1 (2026-05), 3 (2026-06), zero (2026-07). Separately confirmed while writing that query, still unfixed: `PublicLibraryRepositoryImpl`'s SQL-level official-author predicate checks admin-role only, missing the email-based check `PublicProfileService.isOfficialAuthor` (Java) uses — the public library's own OFFICIAL/COMMUNITY filter likely mis-buckets the owner's personally-authored public notes as community today | **[DECISION — made 2026-07-28] + [CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11].** Checked against the 2026-07-28 bootstrap test and passes clause 1 (Knowledge Impact is the only lever that rewards publishing — waiting for publish-rate evidence before building the thing meant to move publish rate is circular); clause 2 (one release slot, reversible — a dashboard feature, no schema lock-in beyond the new event); clause 3 requires the checkpoint itself to be a conditional rate, not the same 3-creator raw count re-run — see below. Kill criterion: a continued-zero or still-collapsed community-publish rate on 2026-09-11 settles "can we create creators" for good, no further deliberation. `09`'s "Answers to the memo's 12 questions" and "Risks to avoid, if/when this is un-parked" sections are the design brief once scoped: passive/pull dashboard (not notifications), retrospective + aggregate framing ("helped," never "ranked"), weight downstream signal (led to N study sessions) over raw view/copy counts, nothing comparative/ranked/real-time/identifying across creators, private-to-the-creator-themselves, no streaks/badges/leaderboards, optional low-frequency opt-in digest reusing the existing Email Preferences category system. On a 3-creator base specifically, absolute per-creator counts ("helped 4 learners") read very differently depending on framing — treat the low-N reality as a design input, not a reason to wait. **Scoping requirement, not optional:** ship a conditional-rate `AnalyticsEventType` (did a creator who viewed their impact dashboard publish again within N days) in the same release — re-running the raw 3-creator-count query in September is the same unmeasurable-checkpoint failure v0.48.0's digest lift already hit; see the gate-types note above | 2026-07-28, kicked off as `v0.62.0` same day; shipped and signed off 2026-07-29 — see `RELEASES.md` v0.62.0 and `docs/releases/v0.62.0.md`. The 2026-09-11 checkpoint remains open, not yet run. |

Adding values to a 12.7%-adopted list with four dead entries would have added more unused rows. **Engineering Economics: the floor is not close to met** — 7 Economics notes exist, all unclassified, and 3 are `Senior High – ABM` (high-school economics, a different subject), so there are effectively **zero** engineering-economics notes; `ADR-001`'s floor is ~10 whose treatment cannot be represented. **What to do instead:** ship descriptions beside the existing `<select>` (~40 LOC, zero schema), classify the 286-note backlog that already has an obvious home, and file Engineering Economics under `Engineering Mathematics` per `08:57`. **Domain Categories declined on evidence** — one curator (one `ADMIN`; the single `TEACHER` owns 4 public notes). **Revisit only if** `ACCOUNTANCY`/`NURSING` remain at zero after descriptions ship *and* a body of notes accumulates whose treatment demonstrably cannot be represented — the first would mean curator time, not taxonomy. **⚠️ The two code-level findings survive this verdict and bind any future attempt:** the `isQuantitativeContext` label substring-match (silently disables computation guidance) and label-is-prompt-payload (rename rewrites history). Funnel numbers: `docs/claude-plans/v0.72.1-activation-read.sql` | **Design direction only — nothing scoped, no version opened.** The evidence: **132 learners, 35.2% of all signups**, verify their email and never finish onboarding — the largest single drop anywhere in the funnel. The owner's framing is that onboarding reads as a settings wizard and should instead be the first thing that communicates what NoteLib is. **Three findings from the design pass change the shape of the work.** (1) **The post-generation flow is not what the brief assumed**: onboarding does not auto-start Quick Review (that concern was never real), but it also does not land the learner on their Study Pack — it ends on a three-CTA completion screen, whose first CTA already resolves to `/study-packs/{id}`. So the recommendation is *delete an interruption*, not *add a celebration*, and it removes a screen without removing a step. (2) **Universal onboarding as proposed would break the flow**: step 4 calls `/notes/generate` and `/notes/{id}/generate`, both of which call `requireEmailVerified`, so running onboarding before verification 403s at the exact moment it promises the first Study Pack. (3) **Steps 3 and 4 are copy and layout over shipped machinery** (the `v0.71.0` slice-5 Intent Router), so those asks are far cheaper than the brief implies | **`[DECISION]`** — and the decision is *on what grounds*, not whether the defect is real. **This work cannot be justified as a retention lever and must not be scoped as one.** `v0.72.1` measured that activation is already **52.2%**, which structurally caps activation-volume work below the retention lever regardless of the retention rate; the volume hypothesis was tested against a pre-committed rule and failed. The legitimate justification is **comprehension and first impression** — 132 people meet the product and leave before understanding it — which is a real reason and a different one. **Recorded plainly because this is the third justification offered for overlapping work:** it was proposed as a retention lever (read killed it), then as activation-volume recovery (arithmetic killed it), now as comprehension. The owner should weigh that pattern knowingly rather than have it pass silently. **Overlaps the Onboarding Intent Router row above — C8 and C9 are the same defects this brief reaches independently from a positioning angle; scope them once, not twice.** **One thing no read can currently answer:** *which* step the 132 abandon on — `profile_type` persists only at the final step and clean step events start 2026-07-28 (n≈5). The design pass is therefore whole-flow by necessity; if a narrower scope is wanted, instrument first — the events already exist and the denominator grows on its own  **SHIPPED as `v0.73.0`, signed off and deployed 2026-08-12.** Eight of ten planned items landed; **item 3 (removing the exam-date question) was never built** — found by the pre-signoff pressure test rather than by the per-item workflow, which had no step that re-read the scope list. **On review 2026-08-12 the item was decided against and closed**, because the post-session prompt that justified removal reaches only session-completers while onboarding reaches everyone; the claim was reworded to "one *required* question per screen" instead. **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]` — deploy-relative (deployed 2026-08-12), owner may override.** **Metric:** onboarding completion rate for signups created ON OR AFTER the deploy, against the **62.4%** (234/375) baseline — computed from `users.onboarding_completed_at`, never from `ONBOARDING_V2_COMPLETED`, per the `AGENTS.md` rule. **Cohort-scoping is load-bearing:** the redesign only reaches learners who onboard after deploy, so an all-time read would be diluted by the pre-deploy population and understate any effect. **Denominator is adequate, unlike the retention checkpoints** — signups run ~120/month, so ~120 is readable at 30 days; this is a rate over signups, not a cohort of 31. **Kill criterion:** if post-deploy completion is not measurably above 62.4%, the comprehension hypothesis this release was justified on is **unsupported** — reopen the framing rather than iterating on further onboarding polish. **If completion DROPS, the 5→8 screen split is the first suspect**, and the per-step funnel shipped as item 10 is what localises it. **Instrumentation verified emitting:** `ONBOARDING_V2_STEP_VIEWED` fires per screen with nine distinct `step_name`s, and the admin funnel reads completion from the `users` table. **One measurement wrinkle, recorded:** the step aggregation is unwindowed (a Known Limitation), so post-deploy scoping needs a windowed query or a delta against the figures recorded at signoff. | 2026-08-12 |

The *integrity* half of the rationale is code-verified and owes nothing — the answer key demonstrably existed. **The *progression* half is the falsifiable one:** that gating the saved questions behind a perfect score improves the study journey rather than stranding people. **Instrumentation verified emitting, not merely declared:** `QUICK_REVIEW_MASTERED` (`QuickReviewSessionService`), `STUDY_PACK_QUIZ_UNLOCKED` (same, once per learner+pack, now firing on unknown prior state too), `STUDY_PACK_QUIZ_TAB_OPENED_AFTER_UNLOCK` (`private-note-detail-page-client.tsx`) | **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-12]`** — deploy+30 (deployed 2026-08-13). **Primary:** among learners starting Quick Review post-deploy, the fraction reaching a verified-perfect score — the unlock rate. **Kill criterion:** a low unlock rate means the gate is a wall rather than a progression → **relax it to "any completed Quick Review"**, which this release's own reasoning already establishes is sufficient for integrity; do not iterate on lock copy instead. **⚠️ Read the secondary metric as the real test.** The pre-deploy `a3` read showed **67.2% of sessions already scored perfect** and 92.0% reached `>= 4/5`, so the unlock rate will very likely come back high and the kill criterion is unlikely to fire — a high rate mostly confirms that perfect scores are common, not that the progression works. **Secondary: does an unlocked learner ever open the Quiz tab?** That tests whether the reward is *wanted* rather than merely reachable. **⚠️ WINDOW SPLIT BY THE `v0.78.0` DEPLOY — recorded 2026-08-14, BEFORE the read, and the timing is the point.** `v0.78.0` fills the mastered result screen's empty secondary slot with a next-Study-Plan-item suggestion, which competes for the click this secondary metric measures. **The metric is UNCHANGED and the kill criterion is UNCHANGED** — what changes is that September's reader must compare **within** sub-windows (pre- and post-`v0.78.0` deploy), not across the discontinuity. **The confound is real but marginal, and that was verified rather than assumed:** `post-session-next-step.tsx` carries **no Quiz-tab link and no unlock affordance** — unlock discovery happens on Note Detail (`private-note-detail-page-client.tsx`, which is where `STUDY_PACK_QUIZ_TAB_OPENED_AFTER_UNLOCK` fires), and the mastered branch's **primary** action has routed away to `Take a Challenge` since `v0.74.0` shipped it in the same release that declared this checkpoint. A secondary action beside an already-diverting primary changes the degree of diversion, not its kind. **A suppression gate was considered and rejected:** showing the suggestion only on repeat mastered sessions would protect the first impression but not this metric, since *"ever opens"* is precisely what later sessions decide — partial protection at the cost of real coupling. **Denominator clause:** too small to read is itself the finding, not grounds to extend | 2026-08-14 |

do not re-send the brief for consultation despite its "consultation-prompt" filename. **Grade the settled items honestly:** the mastery rulings are explicit owner decisions; locking the tab, keeping Challenge open, expressing the progression as a locked tab, and shipping the "Review the Notes" item are settled *by adoption* — they were carried in the returned proposal, not ruled on individually. **Two prior open items closed:** (a) the lock copy was contradicting the gate (*"Complete Quick Review to unlock"* while the gate was perfect) and has been rewritten to name the real condition and point a 4/5 learner at Redo Mistakes; (b) **the "Quick Review length varies by pack" concern is retracted for anything generated under current code, with ONE CHECK STILL OUTSTANDING.** Newly generated quizzes are always exactly 5 — `schema.json` pins `minItems/maxItems: 5`, `STUDY_PACK_QUIZ_QUESTION_COUNT = 5` (`OpenAiLlmStudyPackService.java:64`), and generation is rejected at a different count (`:432`). **But Quick Review takes `totalQuestions` straight from the stored row** (`QuickReviewSessionService.java:98`, no slicing), and that validation only landed **2026-03-18** (`c78ee9f1`) — older packs, and remixes/copies descended from them (`ShareService.java:90`, `NoteService.java:401`), were never subject to it. **Run `docs/claude-plans/v0.74.0-quiz-length-check.sql` before writing learner-facing copy:** a single row of 5 makes "Score 5/5" exact; anything else forces length-agnostic wording (*"answer every question correctly"*). **A distinction worth keeping at implementation time:** the *integrity* rationale (do not hand someone the answer key to a test they have not sat) is satisfied by any completed Quick Review, since the first attempt at each question is the genuine assessment `ConceptHealth` records — the perfect-score requirement is the *progression* layer on top, not the integrity fix. **Still open, a scoping call not a design one:** whether "Review the Notes" completes, pauses, or discards the session, since it moves when `QUICK_REVIEW_COMPLETED` fires and that event feeds the admin funnel's value-loop metric — decide before writing that item's Codex prompt. **One sub-item is time-blocked:** promoting Challenge to the primary CTA on mastery moves the `>= 4/5` threshold that the `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-30]` measures, which would destroy that read — the other six sub-items are unblocked | 2026-08-12 |

## Single Program Axis — remove "Primary Course / Program" (ratified 2026-08-05, scoped into `v0.71.0` as **slice 4**)

**An architectural simplification, not a feature.** Ratified after Release B shipped and the two-field model met real use. Binding text lives in `ADR-001` → "Course / Program(s) is the only program concept"; this section is the scoping view.

**Why it was added to `v0.71.0` after kickoff — a deliberate exception, not drift.** Signoff *is* deploy, and two things that make this change free expire at exactly that moment: **no one has learned the two-field model** (V107 has never reached production, so teachers would otherwise learn it and then re-learn it), and **no multi-program note exists anywhere** (so the Domain Context requirement lands with zero violations and no backfill). Sequencing it after signoff would also mean the expensive whole-release pressure test runs against an intermediate architecture and then again against the real one. It simplifies what this release built rather than extending it.

**All four note-creating surfaces become many-valued — owner ruling 2026-08-05.** Note Editor, Note Detail inline panel, Create-from-topic, and Bulk Generate. The narrower "editor surfaces only" option was offered and declined. **Known consequence, accepted:** because the Domain Context requirement keys on program count, a Bulk Generate batch spanning several programs now also requires a Domain Context — bulk authoring changes from "pick a program, generate" to "pick programs *and* a Domain Context."

**The reframing that settled it.** Every axis owns exactly one responsibility — Subject owns *what*, Domain Context owns *how it is authored*, Note Learner Level owns *how deeply*, Course / Program(s) owns *where it is discovered*. A "primary" program owns **none** of them; it is simply whichever program came first, an artifact of a schema that used to be single-valued. So the question was never "what should replace primary?" but **"should a concept with no remaining architectural responsibility exist?"** — and it should not.

**What ships when this is scoped:**

- **One picker, `Course / Program(s)`**, many-valued, catalog-backed, required. No separate Applicable Programs section, no primary, no synchronisation between two fields. Helper text carries the capability: *"Choose one or more programs this note applies to. Adding multiple programs lets one note serve several curricula instead of creating duplicates."*
- **Domain Context is generation readiness, not note validity, when a note has more than one program.** The multi-program/null-context state may be saved; every note-to-Study-Pack generation entry rejects it server-side before status, quota, rate-limit, or LLM side effects. A single-program note keeps the single-value fallback. **Sending the program list to the LLM is explicitly rejected** — the prompt's domain constraint is unsatisfiable given several disciplines, which is the founding reason Domain Context exists. Revisiting needs an R4-style read first.
- **Note Detail header stops implying a primary.** One line — *"Applicable to N programs"* — opening a popover (desktop) / bottom sheet (mobile) with the full list. **Same viewer on public note detail**, where it earns most: a learner arriving from a shared link never filtered by anything, so "does this fit my program?" is live for them. A single-program note just names it, with no control.
- **Library cards keep the count**, not names. The viewer on detail is what makes that defensible rather than lossy — names stay one tap away. Recommendation, not yet accepted: see the open question below.
- **`notes.course_program` is NOT dropped, and is NOT legacy.** Corrected 2026-08-05 after the authoring-UX pass: it is **the personal-notes program field** and is still written by learner authoring. `note_course_program` is the curated-content axis. An earlier framing in this section and in ADR-001 called it a frozen, write-never compatibility column with an exit condition — that assumed every author would be catalog-restricted, which is not the product. Full reasoning in ADR-001 → "Two authoring modes".
- **Two authoring modes, one gating line.** Curators (Teacher/Admin) get **catalog-only** programs and **one or many**; learners get **free text** and **exactly one**. Cardinality and vocabulary follow the same condition deliberately — splitting them differently would make the model harder to explain than either split alone. Multi-program authoring is a **curation act**, not a permission tier, and must be documented that way.

**Do this while it is free.** `V107` yields exactly one join row per note and has not reached production, so **no multi-program note exists anywhere**. The Domain Context requirement therefore lands with zero pre-existing violations and no backfill. That window closes the moment curators start authoring multi-program notes.

**Migration risks already identified** — none is a blocker, all need handling: live public shareable slug URLs for catalog-excluded values; public search matching `course_program` text; `users.course_program` as the last link in the domain fallback chain; and the irreversibility of dropping any column, which is why none is dropped here.

**Open question, not yet decided:** whether library cards should show program names (owner's instinct: `Civil Engineering • Mechanical Engineering • +2`) or the count (engineering recommendation: names read as a second identity beside the Subject badge, are largely redundant once a learner has filtered by program, and force an arbitrary truncation choice). A GPT consultation prompt covering this and the architecture is at `22-remove-primary-program-gpt-consultation-prompt.md`.

## v0.85.0 — Domain Signal Integrity (Released, base branch `releases/v0.85.0`)

**⚠️ Status corrected at the `v0.97.0` kickoff, 2026-08-29** — this heading read *In Progress* for **eleven releases**, found by `docs/claude-plans/claude-docs-staleness-audit.md`. **⚠️ The per-version prose sections below stop here, at `v0.85.0`, while the project is on `v0.97.0`.** The **Current Release Baseline** and the **Backlog Index** above are current and are the authoritative surfaces; these per-version sections lapsed rather than being discontinued by decision. **Nothing below is maintained — read it as history, and do not treat a missing section as a missing release.** Whether to resume or formally retire the practice is an open one-line decision, indexed with the audit that found it.

Kicked off 2026-08-17, cut from `main` after `v0.84.0` merged and deployed. Origin: the **Domain Context Catalog** proposal (2026-08-17), which a cold-context agent assessed and recommended declining — and which, while auditing the enum's real responsibilities, surfaced a defect much larger than itself. Assessment: `docs/claude-plans/domain-context-catalog-assessment.md`. Sizing: `docs/claude-plans/quantitative-context-coverage-read.sql`.

### Planned scope

1. **A declared per-value quantitative property**, replacing the substring guess. Not a longer keyword list — that keeps guessing. Must not remove guidance from any value that already matches.
2. **Domain Context descriptions beside the authoring select** — copy only, zero schema. The subject lists already exist in `canonical-knowledge-architecture-out/08-…:56-67` and appear nowhere in the product.
3. **Two `ADR-001` factual corrections** — the "single badge" and "vocabulary learners see" claims are both false.

### Explicitly out of scope — anti-drift

- **The Domain Context Catalog, admin CRUD, Domain Categories, prompt-hint fields, and any new enum value** including `General Engineering`. Declined on evidence 2026-08-17.
- **Changing what an existing value resolves to.** `effectiveAuthoringDomain` still returns `getLabel()` — the label is the prompt payload, so changing it would rewrite what past generations were told.
- **The enum stays an enum**, so `@Enumerated(EnumType.STRING)` and the three `Enum.valueOf` projection paths are untouched.
- **Any migration.**

Fixes two `NoteEntity` insert paths that never set the `NOT NULL` `target_profile_type` column, 500ing `/study` paste-text, image upload, confirm-text and share-remix **after** the LLM call has billed quota. Pre-existing since 2026-03-21; surfaced by `v0.83.0`'s cold-context pressure test.

### Planned scope

2. **Tests that would have caught it** — assert the **persisted** value through the real paths, including a `BOARD_EXAM` profile where a constant and a derivation diverge.

### Explicitly out of scope — anti-drift
